     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Stage 3: place each definition's result in the 2024 DORA performance levels.
     3	
     4	The levels come from the 2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report (v. 2024.3,
     5	page 13). They describe survey clusters, not official thresholds, so this is
     6	the reading a dashboard would show, not a verdict on the projects.
     7	Output: results/tiers.json and results/summary.md.
     8	"""
     9	import json
    10	import sys
    11	from pathlib import Path
    12	
    13	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    14	WEEKS_PER_MONTH = 52.1775 / 12
    15	WEEKS_PER_SIX_MONTHS = 52.1775 / 2
    16	
    17	DF_LABELS = {
    18	    "every_tag": "every tag (release candidates too)",
    19	    "final_tags": "every final release tag",
    20	    "release_days": "days with a final release",
    21	    "default_branch_landings": "every landing on the default branch",
    22	}
    23	LT_LABELS = {
    24	    "commit_author_date_first_tag": "commit author date to first tag (candidates too)",
    25	    "commit_author_date": "commit author date to final release",
    26	    "commit_committer_date": "commit committer date to final release",
    27	    "commit_author_date_without_backports": "commit author date to final release, backports left out",
    28	    "change_from_landing": "landing on default branch to final release (per change)",
    29	    "change_from_first_commit": "first commit of the change to final release (per change)",
    30	    "landing_is_deploy": "first commit to landing, if landing is the deploy (per change)",
    31	}
    32	CFR_LABELS = {
    33	    "hotfix_within_7_days": "next patch on the same line within 7 days",
    34	    "ships_a_revert": "release ships any revert commit",
    35	    "shipped_change_later_reverted": "a later release reverts a change it shipped",
    36	}
    37	
    38	
    39	def df_level(per_week):
    40	    if per_week > 7:
    41	        return "Elite"
    42	    if per_week >= 1:
    43	        return "High"
    44	    if per_week >= 1 / WEEKS_PER_MONTH:
    45	        return "Medium"
    46	    if per_week >= 1 / WEEKS_PER_SIX_MONTHS:
    47	        return "Low"
    48	    return "below Low"
    49	
    50	
    51	def lt_level(hours):
    52	    if hours is None:
    53	        return "n/a"
    54	    if hours < 24:
    55	        return "Elite"
    56	    if hours < 24 * 7:
    57	        return "High"
    58	    if hours < 24 * 30:
    59	        return "Medium"
    60	    if hours < 24 * 182.5:
    61	        return "Low"
    62	    return "below Low"
    63	
    64	
    65	def fmt_hours(h):
    66	    if h is None:
    67	        return "n/a"
    68	    if h < 48:
    69	        return f"{h:.1f} h"
    70	    return f"{h / 24:.1f} d"
    71	
    72	
    73	def main():
    74	    metrics = json.loads((ROOT / "results" / "metrics.json").read_text())
    75	    out = {"source": "2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report, v. 2024.3, p. 13",
    76	           "metrics_code_hash": metrics["metrics_code_hash"], "repos": []}
    77	    md = ["# Results", "", f"Window: {metrics['repos'][0]['weeks']} weeks. "
    78	          f"Levels: 2024 DORA report, p. 13. Code: extract {metrics['extract_code_hash']}, "
    79	          f"metrics {metrics['metrics_code_hash']}.", ""]
    80	    for r in metrics["repos"]:
    81	        rec = {"repo": r["repo"], "merge_style": r["merge_style"], "df": {}, "lt": {}}
    82	        md += [f"## {r['repo']} ({r['merge_style']}, head {r['head'][:12]})", "",
    83	               "| Deploy means | Deploys | Per week | Level |", "| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |"]
    84	        for k, v in r["deployment_frequency"].items():
    85	            lvl = df_level(v["per_week"])
    86	            rec["df"][k] = {**v, "level": lvl}
    87	            md.append(f"| {DF_LABELS[k]} | {v['count']} | {v['per_week']:.2f} | {lvl} |")
    88	        md += ["", "| Lead time measured from | Changes | Median | p75 | Level (median) |",
    89	               "| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |"]
    90	        for k, v in r["lead_time"].items():
    91	            lvl = lt_level(v["median_h"])
    92	            rec["lt"][k] = {**v, "level": lvl}
    93	            md.append(f"| {LT_LABELS[k]} | {v['n']} | {fmt_hours(v['median_h'])} | {fmt_hours(v['p75_h'])} | {lvl} |")
    94	        md += ["", "| A release failed if | Failed | Of | Rate |", "| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |"]
    95	        for k, v in r["change_failure_rate"].items():
    96	            md.append(f"| {CFR_LABELS[k]} | {v['failed']} | {v['of']} | {v['rate'] * 100:.1f}% |")
    97	        rec["cfr"] = r["change_failure_rate"]
    98	        rec["recovery"] = r["recovery_time"]
    99	        md += ["", "Recovery time (median): " + ", ".join(
   100	            f"{CFR_LABELS[k]}: {fmt_hours(v['median_h'])} (n={v['n']})" for k, v in r["recovery_time"].items()), ""]
   101	        df_levels = sorted({v["level"] for v in rec["df"].values()})
   102	        lt_levels = sorted({v["level"] for v in rec["lt"].values()})
   103	        rec["df_levels_reached"] = df_levels
   104	        rec["lt_levels_reached"] = lt_levels
   105	        md += [f"Deployment frequency levels reached: {', '.join(df_levels)}. "
   106	               f"Lead time levels reached: {', '.join(lt_levels)}.", ""]
   107	        out["repos"].append(rec)
   108	    (ROOT / "results" / "tiers.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2) + "\n")
   109	    (ROOT / "results" / "summary.md").write_text("\n".join(md) + "\n")
   110	    print("\n".join(md))
   111	    return 0
   112	
   113	
   114	if __name__ == "__main__":
   115	    sys.exit(main())
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Treeless clones: every commit and tag, no file contents. The metrics only read
     3	# commit metadata, so history stays complete for the window at a fraction of the size.
     4	set -euo pipefail
     5	cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
     6	mkdir -p data/repos results
     7	python3 - <<'PY' > /tmp/dora-repos.txt
     8	import json
     9	for r in json.load(open("repos.json"))["repos"]:
    10	    print(r["name"], r["url"], r["branch"])
    11	PY
    12	echo "{" > results/clones.json.tmp
    13	first=1
    14	while read -r name url branch; do
    15	  dir="data/repos/$name"
    16	  if [ -d "$dir" ]; then
    17	    git -C "$dir" fetch -q --tags origin "$branch"
    18	  else
    19	    git clone -q --filter=tree:0 --no-checkout "$url" "$dir"
    20	  fi
    21	  git -C "$dir" fetch -q --tags origin
    22	  head=$(git -C "$dir" rev-parse "origin/$branch")
    23	  commits=$(git -C "$dir" rev-list --count "origin/$branch")
    24	  tags=$(git -C "$dir" tag | wc -l)
    25	  [ $first -eq 1 ] || echo "," >> results/clones.json.tmp
    26	  first=0
    27	  printf '  "%s": {"url": "%s", "branch": "%s", "head": "%s", "commits_on_branch": %s, "tags": %s, "fetched_at": "%s"}' \
    28	    "$name" "$url" "$branch" "$head" "$commits" "$tags" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" >> results/clones.json.tmp
    29	  echo "$name: $head ($commits commits on $branch, $tags tags)"
    30	done < /tmp/dora-repos.txt
    31	echo "" >> results/clones.json.tmp
    32	echo "}" >> results/clones.json.tmp
    33	mv results/clones.json.tmp results/clones.json
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Cross-check stage 1 against plain git on random samples (seeded).
     3	
     4	1. The side commits a merge brought in must equal
     5	   `git rev-list --no-merges <first parent>..<merge>`.
     6	2. The first final release that ships a commit must equal the earliest final
     7	   tag that `git tag --contains` lists for it.
     8	Output: results/method-checks.txt. Exits 1 on any mismatch.
     9	"""
    10	import gzip
    11	import json
    12	import random
    13	import re
    14	import subprocess
    15	import sys
    16	from pathlib import Path
    17	
    18	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    19	SEED = 7
    20	
    21	
    22	def git(repo, *args):
    23	    return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(ROOT / "data" / "repos" / repo), *args],
    24	                          check=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    25	
    26	
    27	def load(repo):
    28	    with gzip.open(ROOT / "results" / "extract" / f"{repo}.json.gz", "rt") as f:
    29	        return json.load(f)
    30	
    31	
    32	def main():
    33	    rng = random.Random(SEED)
    34	    start = None
    35	    lines, failures = [], 0
    36	    for repo in ("kubernetes", "prometheus"):
    37	        ex = load(repo)
    38	        if start is None:
    39	            from datetime import datetime
    40	            start = int(datetime.fromisoformat(ex["window"]["start"]).timestamp())
    41	        merges = [m for m in ex["landings"] if len(m["parents"]) > 1 and m["ct"] >= start]
    42	        ok = 0
    43	        for m in rng.sample(merges, 25):
    44	            want = set(git(repo, "rev-list", "--no-merges", f"{m['parents'][0]}..{m['sha']}").split())
    45	            got = {c["sha"] for c in m["brought"]}
    46	            ok += want == got
    47	        failures += 25 - ok
    48	        lines.append(f"{repo}: side commits match git rev-list for {ok} of 25 random merges")
    49	    for repo in ("kubernetes", "go", "uv"):
    50	        ex = load(repo)
    51	        finals = {t["name"]: t for t in ex["all_tags"] if not t["prerelease"]}
    52	        pairs = [(c["sha"], d["name"]) for d in ex["deploys"] if not d["prerelease"]
    53	                 for c in d["commits_vs_finals"] if len(c["parents"]) == 1]
    54	        ok = 0
    55	        for sha, name in rng.sample(pairs, 15):
    56	            tags = [finals[t] for t in git(repo, "tag", "--contains", sha).split() if t in finals]
    57	            first = min(tags, key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["name"]))["name"] if tags else None
    58	            ok += first == name
    59	        failures += 15 - ok
    60	        lines.append(f"{repo}: first final release matches git tag --contains for {ok} of 15 random commits")
    61	    text = "\n".join(lines) + "\n"
    62	    (ROOT / "results" / "method-checks.txt").write_text(text)
    63	    print(text, end="")
    64	    return 1 if failures else 0
    65	
    66	
    67	if __name__ == "__main__":
    68	    sys.exit(main())
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# One change, three merge buttons, three lead times.
     3	# Two commits are written on Monday and Wednesday, and the change lands on
     4	# Friday. Every date is fixed, so the output is the same on every machine.
     5	set -euo pipefail
     6	tmp=$(mktemp -d)
     7	trap 'rm -rf "$tmp"' EXIT
     8	export GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null
     9	
    10	at() { GIT_AUTHOR_DATE="$1" GIT_COMMITTER_DATE="$1" "${@:2}"; }
    11	
    12	setup() {
    13	  git init -q -b main "$1"
    14	  cd "$1"
    15	  git config user.name dev
    16	  git config user.email dev@example.com
    17	  echo base > README && git add README
    18	  at "2026-09-07T09:00:00Z" git commit -q -m "base"
    19	  git checkout -q -b feature
    20	  echo one > feature.txt && git add feature.txt
    21	  at "2026-09-07T10:00:00Z" git commit -q -m "feature: part 1"
    22	  echo two >> feature.txt && git add feature.txt
    23	  at "2026-09-09T15:00:00Z" git commit -q -m "feature: part 2"
    24	  git checkout -q main
    25	  # Something else lands first, so the branch is behind.
    26	  echo fix > fix.txt && git add fix.txt
    27	  at "2026-09-10T12:00:00Z" git commit -q -m "unrelated fix"
    28	}
    29	
    30	land="2026-09-11T16:00:00Z"   # Friday: the change lands on main
    31	
    32	lead() {
    33	  # The commits the change put on main, then lead time from the earliest
    34	  # author date and from the earliest committer date to the landing.
    35	  local style=$1 range=$2 last
    36	  last=$(git log -1 --format=%ct main)
    37	  echo "$style:"
    38	  git log --no-merges --format='  %s | author %aI | committer %cI' "$range"
    39	  local a c
    40	  a=$(git log --no-merges --format=%at "$range" | sort -n | head -1)
    41	  c=$(git log --no-merges --format=%ct "$range" | sort -n | head -1)
    42	  printf '  lead time from author date %5.1f h, from committer date %5.1f h\n\n' \
    43	    "$(echo "($last - $a) / 3600" | bc -l)" "$(echo "($last - $c) / 3600" | bc -l)"
    44	}
    45	
    46	( setup "$tmp/merge"
    47	  at "$land" git merge -q --no-ff feature -m "Merge feature"
    48	  lead "merge commit" "main^1..main" )
    49	
    50	( setup "$tmp/squash"
    51	  at "$land" git merge -q --squash feature >/dev/null 2>&1
    52	  at "$land" git commit -q -m "feature (squashed)"
    53	  lead "squash" "main~1..main" )
    54	
    55	( setup "$tmp/rebase"
    56	  git checkout -q feature
    57	  GIT_COMMITTER_DATE="$land" git rebase -q main
    58	  git checkout -q main
    59	  git merge -q --ff-only feature
    60	  lead "rebase" "main~2..main" )

/bin/bash -lc "nl -ba /tmp/claude-1000/-home-biliev-projects-devops-daily/f84689a1-064c-4f2a-9937-e3b6665620ab/scratchpad/wt-dora/content/posts/dora-metrics-from-git-history.md | sed -n '200,221p'; rg --files -g '"'!data/repos/**'"' -g '"'!results/extract/**'"' -g '"'!*.pdf'"'; nl -ba results/tiers.json | sed -n '1,240p'" in /home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git
 succeeded in 0ms:
   200	
   201	Recovery time inherits all of this. Under the hotfix rule it is the time to the next patch: 2.9 days for uv, 5.2 for Prometheus, 6.0 for Go. It can never be longer than 7 days, because the rule only counts a failure when the fix came within 7 days. The number measures the rule, not the recovery.
   202	
   203	## What a dashboard adds, and what it does not
   204	
   205	The script shows what Git alone supports. A commercial engineering-metrics tool such as LinearB, Swarmia, Jellyfish, DX, Sleuth or Faros AI adds real data on top:
   206	
   207	- **When the work started.** The forge API knows when a pull request was opened and when its first commit was pushed, which is exactly what a squash merge removes from Git.
   208	- **Real deploy events.** A CD pipeline knows when a version reached production, per environment. A tag is a stand-in.
   209	- **Real failures.** Linking deploys to incidents, rollbacks and pages measures failure directly instead of inferring it from reverts or patch numbers.
   210	- **Ownership and trends.** Mapping repos and paths to services and teams, and recomputing the same way every week.
   211	
   212	What a tool does not remove is the choice of definition. Before you compare a number across teams or tools, ask which event counts as a deploy, which timestamp starts lead time, whether backports and release candidates count, and what counts as a failure. If the answers differ, the numbers do not compare.
   213	
   214	## What we could not conclude
   215	
   216	- **These projects do not deploy.** Tags stand in for deploys. The results test the definitions, not the projects.
   217	- **The levels are survey clusters.** The report presents them as groups of answers, and the band edges are our reading.
   218	- **The failure numbers are small.** The strict revert rule found one failure in three of the four projects, so its rates move a lot with one release.
   219	- **Unshipped work is left out.** Lead time counts only commits that reached a release before the window closed. Go's work since the go1.27 freeze is missing, which pulls its medians down.
   220	- **Tag dates differ.** uv and Go use lightweight tags, dated by the tagged commit. Kubernetes and Prometheus use annotated tags, dated by the tagger. A release can be published hours after its tag.
   221

README.md:7:Compute the four DORA metrics (deployment frequency, change lead time, change failure rate, recovery time) from plain Git history, once per definition, and see how far the definition alone moves the result. It needs `git`, `python3` (standard library only) and `bc`. A run took 56 seconds on a Raspberry Pi 5, and the treeless clones of the four repos take 134 MB.

 succeeded in 0ms:
e7bc0479c
Replace openssl speed CPU burner in summary_test.go

The openssl speed command added in #138423 causes the ContainerMetrics
cadvisor test to fail on CRI-O conformance (ci-node-crio-conformance)
by exceeding upper bounds for container_fs_writes_total,
container_blkio_device_usage_total and container_memory_failures_total.

Replace with a lightweight dd-based CPU burner that generates CPU load
via syscall overhead without filesystem I/O or memory side effects.
Revert the bound changes to pre-#138423 values. The underlying
UsageNanoCores issue is better addressed at the kubelet level
by #138687.

Signed-off-by: Sascha Grunert <sgrunert@redhat.com>

d1cc61a78
test/images: fix tf-wide-deep build by using models v1.9.0

The wide_deep directory was removed from tensorflow/models after v1.9.0.
Commit f8ca585b666 updated models from v1.9.0 to v2.15.0, breaking the
build because /models-2.15.0/official/wide_deep/data_download.py no
longer exists.

Revert to models v1.9.0 which contains the wide_deep implementation.
The v1.9.0 code uses tf.estimator APIs which are still available in
TensorFlow 2.x.

Fixes: https://prow.k8s.io/view/gs/kubernetes-ci-logs/logs/post-kubernetes-push-e2e-node-perf-tf-wide-deep-test-images/2011957382287986688

Signed-off-by: Davanum Srinivas <davanum@gmail.com>

3ec711f91
cluster/gce/gci: revert PATH change when using custom runc

Commit 6031ff29c144fe16e98f6fcb1d7201634f9a173e
("make containerd download more robust") updated the containerd systemd
unit to use `Environment=PATH=/home/containerd/bin:$PATH`. However,
environment variables are not evaluated in systemd units in that way.
This resulted in containerd running with the literal value
`/home/containerd/bin:$PATH` for its PATH, breaking containerd (it could
not access the apparmor_parser program).

The old way passes the PATH that configure.sh runs with to containerd,
which is not optimal, but at least it doesn't break the tests. Let's
revert to the old way.

5158bdaac
service-ip-alloc: delay ip processing on service recreate

Service storage implements transactions. It creates an IPAddress object first and then creates
the Service object, and if the Service object already exists the complete transaction is
reverted. There can be race conditions when the repair loop picks up the new IPAddress object
for reconciliation before the transaction is reverted. This leads to spurious
IPAddressWrongReference warnings, to suppress these warnings we delay the processing of the new
IPAddress object by 5 seconds. The service allocation creates the IPAddress object before creating
the Service object, we easily identify this scenario when the IPAddress object creation timestamp
is after the Service creation timestamp. We do this only when the IPAddress object is created
recently in order to avoid indefinitely requeue/delay in IPAddress cleanup if for some reason
the service transaction revert fails.

Signed-off-by: Daman Arora <aroradaman@gmail.com>

 succeeded in 0ms:
442a3bf
Version all Azure requests (#21366)

Anonymous requests to an Azure storage account with public access
disabled can return 409 before uv attempts credentials. With uv 0.12.7
and a matching UV_AZURE_ENDPOINT_URL, a wheel download failed: its
anonymous HEAD response had no x-ms-error-code header. The special case
added in 17b195bd714 retries only 409 responses whose error header is
PublicAccessNotPermitted, so it missed this response.

The API version explains the difference. uv previously added
x-ms-version: 2023-11-03 only when signing an authenticated request.
Without that header, an anonymous request uses the account's
DefaultServiceVersion, or a legacy version if that property is unset
[1]. Anonymous HEAD requests to the same affected blob returned:

| x-ms-version | HTTP status | x-ms-error-code |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Omitted | 409 | Absent |
| 2019-07-07 | 409 | PublicAccessNotPermitted |
| 2019-12-12 | 401 | NoAuthenticationInformation |
| 2020-10-02 | 401 | NoAuthenticationInformation |

Add x-ms-version before authentication for requests matching the
configured Azure endpoint, preserving explicitly supplied versions.
Bearer authorization requires 2017-11-09, and anonymous 401 bearer
challenges require 2019-12-12 [2]. The earliest version meeting both
requirements that Azure Stack Hub 2301 and later supports is 2020-10-02;
its next older supported version is 2019-07-07 [3]. The existing 401
handling can then discover credentials and retry with authentication, so
revert the Azure-specific 409 handling from 17b195bd714.

Pinning the request version makes this behavior independent of the
account default. An account owner could change DefaultServiceVersion
instead, but that affects other unversioned clients too [4]. Compatible
endpoints must accept the requested version. The shared API-version
requirement is therefore 2020-10-02, rather than the 2023-11-03
previously used for signed requests.

To reproduce, use a blob in an account with anonymous public access
disabled. The unversioned result depends on the account default; the
explicitly versioned requests isolate the 409-to-401 change:

```sh
blob_url='https://<account>.blob.core.windows.net/<container>/<blob>'
curl -q -sSI --max-time 30 "$blob_url"
curl -q -sSI --max-time 30 -H 'x-ms-version: 2019-07-07' "$blob_url"
curl -q -sSI --max-time 30 -H 'x-ms-version: 2019-12-12' "$blob_url"
curl -q -sSI --max-time 30 -H 'x-ms-version: 2020-10-02' "$blob_url"
```

[1]:
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/storageservices/versioning-for-the-azure-storage-services#requests-via-anonymous-access
[2]:
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/storageservices/authorize-with-azure-active-directory#bearer-challenge
[3]:
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure-stack/user/azure-stack-acs-differences#api-version
[4]:
https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/rest/api/storageservices/set-blob-service-properties

    78	def landings_with_side_commits(repo, branch, since, until):
    79	    """First-parent commits on the default branch, oldest first, each with the
    80	    commits it brought in. A merge brings the commits of its second parent that
    81	    no earlier landing brought; other commits bring only themselves."""
    82	    graph = {c["sha"]: c for c in rev_list(repo, branch, f"--since={since - YEAR}")}
    83	    firsts = rev_list(repo, "--first-parent", "--reverse", branch, f"--since={since}", f"--until={until}")
    84	    mainline = {m["sha"] for m in firsts}
    85	    assigned = set()
    86	    out = []
    87	    for m in firsts:
    88	        brought = []
    89	        if len(m["parents"]) > 1:
    90	            stack = list(m["parents"][1:])
    91	            while stack:
    92	                sha = stack.pop()
    93	                if sha in assigned or sha in mainline or sha not in graph:
    94	                    continue
    95	                assigned.add(sha)
    96	                c = graph[sha]
    97	                if len(c["parents"]) == 1:
    98	                    brought.append({"sha": sha, "at": c["at"], "ct": c["ct"]})
    99	                stack.extend(c["parents"])
   100	        out.append({**m, "brought": brought})
   101	    return out
   102	
   103	
   104	def read_reverts(repo, since):
   105	    fmt = SEP.join(["%H", "%at", "%ct", "%s", "%b"]) + "\x1e"
   106	    reverts = []
   107	    for rec in git(repo, "log", "--all", "-i", "--grep=^Revert", f"--since={since}", f"--format={fmt}").split("\x1e"):
   108	        rec = rec.strip("\n")
   109	        if not rec:
   110	            continue
   111	        sha, at, ct, subject, body = rec.split(SEP, 4)
   112	        target_sha = re.search(r"reverts commit ([0-9a-f]{7,40})", body)
   113	        target_cl = re.search(r"reverts CL (\d+)", body)
   114	        reverts.append({
   115	            "sha": sha, "at": int(at), "ct": int(ct), "subject": subject,
   116	            "target_sha": target_sha.group(1) if target_sha else None,
   117	            "target_cl": target_cl.group(1) if target_cl else None,
   118	        })
   119	    return reverts
   128	    # ---- Change failure rate and recovery (deploy = final release tag) --------
   129	    # 1. Hotfix: the next release on the same major.minor line came within HOTFIX_DAYS.
   130	    by_line = {}
   131	    for t in ex["all_tags"]:
   132	        if not t["prerelease"]:
   133	            by_line.setdefault((t["major"], t["minor"]), []).append(t)
   134	    hotfix_failed, hotfix_recovery = [], []
   135	    for d in finals:
   136	        line = sorted(by_line[(d["major"], d["minor"])], key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["patch"]))
   137	        later = [t for t in line if t["patch"] > d["patch"] and t["ts"] >= d["ts"]]
   138	        if later and later[0]["ts"] - d["ts"] <= HOTFIX_DAYS * DAY:
   139	            hotfix_failed.append(d["name"])
   140	            hotfix_recovery.append(later[0]["ts"] - d["ts"])
   141	
   142	    # 2. The release ships at least one revert commit.
   143	    has_revert = [d["name"] for d in finals
   144	                  if any(c["subject"].lower().startswith("revert") for c in d["commits_vs_finals"])]
   145	
   146	    # 3. A later release reverts a change this release shipped.
   147	    shipped_in = {}
   148	    for d in sorted(finals, key=lambda x: x["ts"]):
   149	        for c in d["commits_vs_finals"]:
   150	            shipped_in.setdefault(c["sha"], d)
   151	    revert_shas = {rv["sha"]: rv for rv in ex["reverts"]}
   152	
   153	    def resolve(rv):
   154	        if rv["target_sha"]:
   155	            for sha in shipped_in:
   156	                if sha.startswith(rv["target_sha"]):
   157	                    return sha
   158	            return None
   159	        if rv["target_cl"]:
   160	            return ex["cl_map"].get(rv["target_cl"])
   161	        return None
   162	
   163	    reverted_failed, reverted_recovery = {}, []
   164	    no_target, target_not_in_earlier_release = 0, 0
   165	    for d in finals:
   166	        for c in d["commits_vs_finals"]:
   167	            rv = revert_shas.get(c["sha"])
   168	            if not rv or len(c["parents"]) > 1:
   169	                continue  # a merge commit that only quotes a revert in its message
   170	            if not rv["target_sha"] and not rv["target_cl"]:
   171	                no_target += 1
   172	                continue
   173	            target = resolve(rv)
   174	            if target is None or target not in shipped_in or shipped_in[target]["ts"] >= d["ts"]:
   175	                # Reverted before it reached a release in the window, or shipped earlier.
   176	                target_not_in_earlier_release += 1
   177	                continue
   178	            origin = shipped_in.get(target)
   179	            if origin is None or origin["ts"] >= d["ts"] or origin["name"] in reverted_failed:
   180	                continue
   181	            reverted_failed[origin["name"]] = d["name"]
   182	            reverted_recovery.append(d["ts"] - origin["ts"])
   183	
    39	def df_level(per_week):
    40	    if per_week > 7:
    41	        return "Elite"
    42	    if per_week >= 1:
    43	        return "High"
    44	    if per_week >= 1 / WEEKS_PER_MONTH:
    45	        return "Medium"
    46	    if per_week >= 1 / WEEKS_PER_SIX_MONTHS:
    47	        return "Low"
    48	    return "below Low"
    49	
    50	
    51	def lt_level(hours):
    52	    if hours is None:
    53	        return "n/a"
    54	    if hours < 24:
    55	        return "Elite"
    56	    if hours < 24 * 7:
    57	        return "High"
    58	    if hours < 24 * 30:
    59	        return "Medium"
    60	    if hours < 24 * 182.5:
    61	        return "Low"
    62	    return "below Low"
    28	| `scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh` | nothing | `results/demo-date-rewrite.txt`: one change landed three ways (merge commit, squash, rebase) and the lead time each one leaves in Git |
    29	| `scripts/check-method.py` | the extracts and the clones | `results/method-checks.txt`: stage 1 cross-checked against plain git on seeded random samples |
    30	
    31	Each stage stamps its output with a hash of its own code and inputs. `02-metrics.py` refuses to mix extracts from different extract code.
    32	
    33	## The definitions
    34	
    35	- **Deploy:** every tag, release candidates included; every final release tag; days with a final release; or every first-parent commit on the default branch ("merge to main is a deploy").
    36	- **Lead time:** from the commit author date, the committer date, the time the change landed on the default branch, or the first commit of the change; to the first tag or to the first final release that contains it. Per commit or per change (one landing on the default branch).
    37	- **Failure:** the next patch on the same `major.minor` line came within 7 days; the release ships any commit whose subject starts with "Revert"; or a later release reverts a change this release shipped.
    38	- **Recovery:** from the failed release to the release that fixed it, for the two failure rules that name one.
    39	
    40	The performance levels come from the 2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report (v. 2024.3, page 13). They describe survey clusters, not official thresholds, and the report gives single values for change failure rate (5%, 20%, 10%, 40%), so `03-tiers.py` only places deployment frequency and lead time.
    41	
    42	## Results
    43	
    44	See `results/summary.md` for every number, and the post for what they mean. In short: each repo reaches two or three different levels for the same metric depending on the definition, and the merge button decides whether Git still knows when the work started.
    45	
    46	## Checks done on the method
    47	
    48	`scripts/check-method.py` (seeded) compares stage 1 with plain git: the side commits each merge brought in against `git rev-list --no-merges <first-parent>..<merge>` on 50 random merges, and the first final release that ships a commit against `git tag --contains` on 45 random commits. All 95 match; see `results/method-checks.txt`.

The post mostly reproduces the saved numbers correctly, but its central comparison changes populations as well as definitions. There are also concrete problems in failure detection and the instructions for using other repositories.

Below, `post` refers to [dora-metrics-from-git-history.md](/tmp/claude-1000/-home-biliev-projects-devops-daily/f84689a1-064c-4f2a-9937-e3b6665620ab/scratchpad/wt-dora/content/posts/dora-metrics-from-git-history.md).

1. **High — “Same commits, seven definitions” is not a like-for-like comparison.**  
   **Locations:** `post:111–126`, `post:148`, `post:253`; [02-metrics.py:76](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:76), [02-metrics.py:100](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:100).  
   Kubernetes’s rows contain **3,286 landings**, **6,812 first-tag commits**, **6,091 first-final commits**, **3,734 grouped changes**, and **5,486 commits after filtering**. Landings are selected by landing date; release rows are selected by release date and can include earlier landings. Changing the stopping event also changes which observations enter the sample. Consequently, **5.2 → 81.9 days** does not isolate clock-definition effects on identical work. Say “same repository history, different definitions and cohorts,” or use a matched cohort. The **76.6 versus 63.1 days** author/committer comparison does use the same commit population.

2. **Medium — Failure rules use different follow-up windows, affecting a headline percentage.**  
   **Locations:** `post:186–191`; [02-metrics.py:130](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:130), [02-metrics.py:148](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:148), [metrics.json:571](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/metrics.json:571).  
   Hotfix detection searches **all tags**, including those after October 1; strict revert detection searches only releases inside the window. The saved uv extract shows `0.12.21` on September 29 counted as failed because `0.12.22` appeared on **October 1 at 23:31 UTC**, outside the window. Restricting this hotfix comparison to in-window fixes gives **87/101 = 86.1%**, rather than **88/101 = 87.1%**. Following failures beyond the window is defensible, but the rules need a consistent, disclosed follow-up policy. Strict revert rates and recovery times also omit unresolved outcomes at the window boundary.

3. **Medium — “Landing on master” includes commits that have no recorded master landing; “per change” does not consistently mean per PR.**  
   **Locations:** `post:117`, `post:120`, `post:150–151`; [02-metrics.py:82](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:82), [README.md:36](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/README.md:36).  
   When a commit lacks a landing lookup, the code still includes it in both per-change metrics, substituting its **committer date** and counting its SHA as a separate change. Kubernetes’s 3,734 observations therefore include the **605 unmatched commits**. The 60.6-day row is a mixture of master-landing and commit-date clocks. Additionally, a rebased ten-commit PR becomes ten first-parent observations, not one change. Describe the grouping precisely. “Never landed on master” also exceeds what the bounded landing lookup proves.

4. **Medium — “25 Kubernetes reverts and 22 uv reverts name no commit” misstates what the parser measured.**  
   **Locations:** `post:197`; [01-extract.py:107](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/01-extract.py:107), [02-metrics.py:170](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:170).  
   These counters mean **neither of two case-sensitive body patterns matched**, not that no target was named. Concrete examples in the saved history:
   - Kubernetes `3ec711f91…` explicitly names commit `6031ff29c144fe16e98f6fcb1d7201634f9a173e`.
   - uv `442a3bfd…` explicitly says to revert handling from `17b195bd714`.

   Both are counted as naming no commit. Furthermore, `--grep=^Revert` searches message-body lines too: Kubernetes `5158bdaac…` matches prose about a transaction being “reverted,” rather than a Git revert. This differs from rule two’s subject-prefix filter. Rename the counter to “unresolved by this parser,” and align the candidate rules. [Git’s documentation confirms that `--grep` searches the log message.](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-log)

5. **Medium — The merge-button conclusion treats configurable timestamps as guaranteed landing events.**  
   **Locations:** `post:172`, `post:180`; [demo-date-rewrite.sh:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh:10), [demo-date-rewrite.sh:50](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh:50), [02-metrics.py:105](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:105).  
   The demo explicitly sets both squash timestamps to Friday and the rebase committer timestamp to Friday. The metric then uses the committer timestamp as “landing.” This demonstrates that workflow, but does not establish that every squash-merging team necessarily looks Elite. Commit timestamps also do not independently record when an existing commit was pushed or fast-forwarded onto the remote branch. Retain the observed uv statistics; qualify the universal claim. Git permits overriding both author-date and committer-date behavior. [Git rebase documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rebase)

6. **Medium — The advertised custom-repository workflow does not work as written.**  
   **Locations:** `post:231–243`; [check-method.py:36](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:36), [check-method.py:49](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:49), [run.sh:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/run.sh:10).  
   Replacing `repos.json` with another repository leaves the mandatory checker hard-coded to Kubernetes, Prometheus, Go, and uv. A fresh custom run fails loading their extracts; an existing checkout checks unrelated old extracts. Separately, `deploy-2026-10-14-1` matches none of the supplied release patterns. The extractor requires numeric version groups plus a fourth capture group, and hotfix detection depends on version ordering. Arbitrary deploy tags require configuration and metric changes, not just the suggested `git tag` command.

7. **Medium — Several factual claims lack recorded evidence.**  
   **Locations:** `post:53`, `post:81`, `post:184`, `post:205–210`; [README.md:7](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/README.md:7).  
   **134 MB**, **56 seconds**, and the **Raspberry Pi 5** benchmark are repeated in the README but have no measurement log or hardware record in the reviewed evidence. Nor do the files establish that these are definitions “all in real use,” the “three rules we see most often,” or capabilities shared by every named vendor. Record the benchmark conditions and cite implementations/product documentation, or present these as illustrative definitions and possible integrations.

8. **Medium — The direction of the unshipped-work bias is asserted without evidence.**  
   **Location:** `post:219`.  
   Excluding unfinished work creates censoring, but does not establish that Go’s observed median is necessarily pulled downward. Recently started, unshipped changes could eventually finish faster than the current median. The files provide neither a counterfactual completed cohort nor a censoring analysis. State that the median describes completed releases and excludes unfinished work.

9. **Low — The extractor misassigns history at its lookback boundary.**  
   **Locations:** [01-extract.py:82](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/01-extract.py:82), [check-method.py:41](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:41).  
   `assigned` starts empty, and traversal stops only at first-parent commits inside the lookback slice. It can therefore absorb ancestors already present before that slice. The saved Kubernetes extract assigns **4,063 brought commits** to merge `35065cb…`; reading its actual first-parent range shows **one**, `6933c1bc2`. The random checks exclude this warm-up period. This can corrupt landing attribution for old work released later. **I found no overlap between those 4,063 commits and this window’s first-final shipments**, so this example does not establish a changed current headline.

10. **Low — Go’s final-release frequency rounds incorrectly.**  
    **Locations:** `post:87`; [summary.md:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/summary.md:10), [02-metrics.py:70](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:70).  
    `30 ÷ (365/7) = 0.575342…`, which rounds to **0.58/week**, not **0.57/week**. Rounding to three decimals before formatting to two produces the discrepancy. The Medium classification is unaffected.

11. **Low — Several labels are more specific than the evidence supports.**  
    **Locations:** `post:26`, `post:50`, `post:153`, `post:195`; [metrics.json:190](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/metrics.json:190), [metrics.json:68](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/metrics.json:68).  
    Kubernetes’s **3,286** observations are **3,135 merge commits plus 151 single-parent landings**. Go also has **eight merge landings**, so its history is not entirely linear. Go’s **69.1 days** is time to the first matching tag, including beta/final tags—not specifically the first rc. And uv does not increment the patch number for *every* release: the results include `0.9.0`, `0.10.0`, and other minor-version transitions.

12. **Low — The percentile helper is not nearest-rank as documented.**  
    **Location:** [02-metrics.py:26](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:26).  
    It uses `round(q/100*n + 0.5)` instead of `ceil(q/100*n)`. For four observations, p75 selects the fourth value instead of the third. This affects saved percentiles, including Go’s four-event hotfix recovery summary; it does not affect medians or median-based tiers.

The remaining displayed counts, percentages, median conversions, demo’s **102 hours**, and recorded **50/50** and **45/45** checks match the evidence. The Go release dates and tag-type claims also match the saved extracts. The extraction and metrics code hashes match the recorded hashes.

The **DORA table is quoted correctly**, including High’s 20% versus Medium’s 10% failure rate. All displayed tiers follow [03-tiers.py:39](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/03-tiers.py:39). Its weekly-average thresholds and 30-/182.5-day boundaries are custom operational interpretations, as the post acknowledges; the supplied report does not prescribe those numerical cutoffs.

No project scripts, tests, or SQL were run.

[Git’s documentation confirms that `--grep` searches the log message.](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-log)

5. **Medium — The merge-button conclusion treats configurable timestamps as guaranteed landing events.**  
   **Locations:** `post:172`, `post:180`; [demo-date-rewrite.sh:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh:10), [demo-date-rewrite.sh:50](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh:50), [02-metrics.py:105](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:105).  
   The demo explicitly sets both squash timestamps to Friday and the rebase committer timestamp to Friday. The metric then uses the committer timestamp as “landing.” This demonstrates that workflow, but does not establish that every squash-merging team necessarily looks Elite. Commit timestamps also do not independently record when an existing commit was pushed or fast-forwarded onto the remote branch. Retain the observed uv statistics; qualify the universal claim. Git permits overriding both author-date and committer-date behavior. [Git rebase documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rebase)

6. **Medium — The advertised custom-repository workflow does not work as written.**  
   **Locations:** `post:231–243`; [check-method.py:36](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:36), [check-method.py:49](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:49), [run.sh:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/run.sh:10).  
   Replacing `repos.json` with another repository leaves the mandatory checker hard-coded to Kubernetes, Prometheus, Go, and uv. A fresh custom run fails loading their extracts; an existing checkout checks unrelated old extracts. Separately, `deploy-2026-10-14-1` matches none of the supplied release patterns. The extractor requires numeric version groups plus a fourth capture group, and hotfix detection depends on version ordering. Arbitrary deploy tags require configuration and metric changes, not just the suggested `git tag` command.

7. **Medium — Several factual claims lack recorded evidence.**  
   **Locations:** `post:53`, `post:81`, `post:184`, `post:205–210`; [README.md:7](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/README.md:7).  
   **134 MB**, **56 seconds**, and the **Raspberry Pi 5** benchmark are repeated in the README but have no measurement log or hardware record in the reviewed evidence. Nor do the files establish that these are definitions “all in real use,” the “three rules we see most often,” or capabilities shared by every named vendor. Record the benchmark conditions and cite implementations/product documentation, or present these as illustrative definitions and possible integrations.

8. **Medium — The direction of the unshipped-work bias is asserted without evidence.**  
   **Location:** `post:219`.  
   Excluding unfinished work creates censoring, but does not establish that Go’s observed median is necessarily pulled downward. Recently started, unshipped changes could eventually finish faster than the current median. The files provide neither a counterfactual completed cohort nor a censoring analysis. State that the median describes completed releases and excludes unfinished work.

9. **Low — The extractor misassigns history at its lookback boundary.**  
   **Locations:** [01-extract.py:82](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/01-extract.py:82), [check-method.py:41](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:41).  
   `assigned` starts empty, and traversal stops only at first-parent commits inside the lookback slice. It can therefore absorb ancestors already present before that slice. The saved Kubernetes extract assigns **4,063 brought commits** to merge `35065cb…`; reading its actual first-parent range shows **one**, `6933c1bc2`. The random checks exclude this warm-up period. This can corrupt landing attribution for old work released later. **I found no overlap between those 4,063 commits and this window’s first-final shipments**, so this example does not establish a changed current headline.

10. **Low — Go’s final-release frequency rounds incorrectly.**  
    **Locations:** `post:87`; [summary.md:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/summary.md:10), [02-metrics.py:70](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:70).  
    `30 ÷ (365/7) = 0.575342…`, which rounds to **0.58/week**, not **0.57/week**. Rounding to three decimals before formatting to two produces the discrepancy. The Medium classification is unaffected.

11. **Low — Several labels are more specific than the evidence supports.**  
    **Locations:** `post:26`, `post:50`, `post:153`, `post:195`; [metrics.json:190](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/metrics.json:190), [metrics.json:68](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/metrics.json:68).  
    Kubernetes’s **3,286** observations are **3,135 merge commits plus 151 single-parent landings**. Go also has **eight merge landings**, so its history is not entirely linear. Go’s **69.1 days** is time to the first matching tag, including beta/final tags—not specifically the first rc. And uv does not increment the patch number for *every* release: the results include `0.9.0`, `0.10.0`, and other minor-version transitions.

12. **Low — The percentile helper is not nearest-rank as documented.**  
    **Location:** [02-metrics.py:26](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:26).  
    It uses `round(q/100*n + 0.5)` instead of `ceil(q/100*n)`. For four observations, p75 selects the fourth value instead of the third. This affects saved percentiles, including Go’s four-event hotfix recovery summary; it does not affect medians or median-based tiers.

The remaining displayed counts, percentages, median conversions, demo’s **102 hours**, and recorded **50/50** and **45/45** checks match the evidence. The Go release dates and tag-type claims also match the saved extracts. The extraction and metrics code hashes match the recorded hashes.

The **DORA table is quoted correctly**, including High’s 20% versus Medium’s 10% failure rate. All displayed tiers follow [03-tiers.py:39](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/03-tiers.py:39). Its weekly-average thresholds and 30-/182.5-day boundaries are custom operational interpretations, as the post acknowledges; the supplied report does not prescribe those numerical cutoffs.

No project scripts, tests, or SQL were run.
# dora-from-git

```bash
./run.sh
```

Compute the four DORA metrics (deployment frequency, change lead time, change failure rate, recovery time) from plain Git history, once per definition, and see how far the definition alone moves the result. It needs `git`, `python3` (standard library only) and `bc`. `scripts/benchmark.sh` records what a run costs: 137 seconds on a Raspberry Pi 4 Model B, with 132 MB of treeless clones (`results/benchmark.txt`).

The repos it measures, one per merge style, over 1 October 2025 to 1 October 2026:

| Repo | Merge style | Deploy record |
| --- | --- | --- |
| kubernetes/kubernetes | merge commits | `vX.Y.Z` tags, alpha, beta and rc tags |
| astral-sh/uv | squash | `X.Y.Z` tags |
| golang/go (go.googlesource.com) | rebase through Gerrit, almost linear history | `goX.Y.Z` tags, beta and rc tags |
| prometheus/prometheus | mixed: about half merge commits, half squash | `vX.Y.Z` tags and rc tags |

Edit `repos.json` to point it at your own repositories. Any Git host works: nothing here calls a forge API.

## What each script does

| Script | Reads | Writes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `scripts/00-clone.sh` | `repos.json` | treeless clones in `data/repos/` (commits and tags, no file contents), `results/clones.json` with the head of each |
| `scripts/01-extract.py` | the clones | `results/extract/<repo>.json.gz`: tags and the commits each one shipped, every first-parent commit on the default branch with the commits it brought in, and every revert with the commit or Gerrit change it names |
| `scripts/02-metrics.py` | the extracts | `results/metrics.json`: every metric under every definition |
| `scripts/03-tiers.py` | `results/metrics.json` | `results/tiers.json` and `results/summary.md`: each result placed in the 2024 DORA performance levels |
| `scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh` | nothing | `results/demo-date-rewrite.txt`: one change landed three ways (merge commit, squash, rebase) and the lead time each one leaves in Git |
| `scripts/check-method.py` | the extracts and the clones | `results/method-checks.txt`: stage 1 cross-checked against plain git on seeded random samples, for every repo in `repos.json` |
| `scripts/benchmark.sh` | the whole pipeline | `results/benchmark.txt`: machine, wall time and clone sizes of one full run |

Each stage stamps its output with a hash of its own code and inputs. `02-metrics.py` refuses to mix extracts from different extract code.

## The definitions

- **Deploy:** every tag, release candidates included; every final release tag; days with a final release; or every first-parent commit on the default branch ("merge to main is a deploy").
- **Lead time:** from the commit author date, the committer date, the time the change landed on the default branch, or the first commit of the change; to the first tag or to the first final release that contains it. Per commit or per change. A change is one landing on the default branch: a merge commit and its side branch count once, each commit of a rebased branch counts on its own, and a commit with no landing on the default branch (a backport) counts on its own from its committer date.
- **Failure:** the next patch on the same `major.minor` line came within 7 days, inside the window; the release ships any commit whose subject starts with "Revert"; or a later release in the window ships a revert whose message names ("reverts commit <hash>" or "reverts CL <number>") a change this release shipped.
- **Recovery:** from the failed release to the release that fixed it, for the two failure rules that name one.

The performance levels come from the 2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report (v. 2024.3, page 13). They describe survey clusters, not official thresholds, and the report gives single values for change failure rate (5%, 20%, 10%, 40%), so `03-tiers.py` only places deployment frequency and lead time.

## Results

See `results/summary.md` for every number, and the post for what they mean. In short: each repo reaches two or three different levels for the same metric depending on the definition, and the merge button decides whether Git still knows when the work started.

## Checks done on the method

`scripts/check-method.py` (seeded) compares stage 1 with plain git: the side commits each merge brought in against `git rev-list --no-merges <first-parent>..<merge>` on 58 random merges, and the first final release that ships a commit against `git tag --contains` on 60 random commits. All match; see `results/method-checks.txt`.

MIT

| every landing on the default branch | 3422 | 65.63 | Elite |

| Lead time measured from | Changes | Median | p75 | Level (median) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |

| commit committer date to final release | 3878 | 112.0 d | 159.1 d | Low |
| commit author date to final release, backports left out | 3415 | 138.0 d | 182.8 d | Low |
| landing on default branch to final release (per change) | 3521 | 110.0 d | 153.7 d | Low |
| first commit of the change to final release (per change) | 3521 | 125.3 d | 172.4 d | Low |
| first commit to landing, if landing is the deploy (per change) | 3422 | 4.1 d | 15.0 d | High |

| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |

| release ships any revert commit | 2 | 30 | 6.7% |

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Stage 1: read commit metadata and tags from each clone into results/extract/<repo>.json.

Only plain git is used, so it works for any host. Nothing here decides what a
deploy or a failure is; stage 2 does that from this file.
"""
import gzip
import hashlib
import json
import re
import subprocess
import sys
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
CONFIG = json.loads((ROOT / "repos.json").read_text())
OUT = ROOT / "results" / "extract"
SEP = "\x1f"
YEAR = 365 * 86400


def git(repo, *args):
    return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), *args], check=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout


def iso_to_ts(value):
    return int(datetime.fromisoformat(value).timestamp())


def code_hash():
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    for f in (Path(__file__), ROOT / "repos.json", ROOT / "results" / "clones.json"):
        h.update(f.read_bytes())
    return h.hexdigest()[:16]


def read_tags(repo, pattern):
    fmt = SEP.join(["%(refname:short)", "%(objecttype)", "%(objectname)", "%(*objectname)", "%(creatordate:iso-strict)"])
    rx = re.compile(pattern)
    tags = []
    for line in git(repo, "for-each-ref", "refs/tags", f"--format={fmt}").splitlines():
        name, otype, obj, peeled, created = line.split(SEP)
        m = rx.match(name)
        if not m:
            continue
        nums = [int(g) if g else 0 for g in m.groups()[:3]]
        tags.append({
            "name": name,
            "annotated": otype == "tag",
            "commit": peeled if otype == "tag" else obj,
            # Annotated: the tagger date. Lightweight: the tagged commit's committer date.
            "ts": iso_to_ts(created),
            "major": nums[0], "minor": nums[1], "patch": nums[2],
            "prerelease": bool(m.groups()[3]),
        })
    tags.sort(key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["name"]))
    return tags


def rev_list(repo, *args):
    """Commits as dicts: sha, parents, author ts, committer ts, subject."""
    fmt = SEP.join(["%H", "%P", "%at", "%ct", "%s"])
    rows = []
    for line in git(repo, "log", f"--format={fmt}", *args).splitlines():
        sha, parents, at, ct, subject = line.split(SEP, 4)
        rows.append({"sha": sha, "parents": parents.split(), "at": int(at), "ct": int(ct), "subject": subject})
    return rows


def shipped(repo, tag, earlier):
    """Commits reachable from tag and from none of the earlier tags."""
    if earlier:
        return rev_list(repo, tag["commit"], "--not", *[t["commit"] for t in earlier])
    return rev_list(repo, tag["commit"])


def landings_with_side_commits(repo, branch, since, until):
    """First-parent commits on the default branch, oldest first, each with the
    commits it brought in. A merge brings the commits of its second parent that
    no earlier landing brought; other commits bring only themselves."""
    graph = {c["sha"]: c for c in rev_list(repo, branch, f"--since={since - YEAR}")}
    firsts = rev_list(repo, "--first-parent", "--reverse", branch, f"--since={since}", f"--until={until}")
    mainline = {m["sha"] for m in firsts}
    # Everything already on the branch before the first landing counts as
    # landed, so the first merge in the slice does not absorb older history.
    assigned = set()
    if firsts:
        stack = list(firsts[0]["parents"][:1])
        while stack:
            sha = stack.pop()
            if sha in assigned or sha not in graph:
                continue
            assigned.add(sha)
            stack.extend(graph[sha]["parents"])
    out = []
    for m in firsts:
        brought = []
        if len(m["parents"]) > 1:
            stack = list(m["parents"][1:])
            while stack:
                sha = stack.pop()
                if sha in assigned or sha in mainline or sha not in graph:
                    continue
                assigned.add(sha)
                c = graph[sha]
                if len(c["parents"]) == 1:
                    brought.append({"sha": sha, "at": c["at"], "ct": c["ct"]})
                stack.extend(c["parents"])
        out.append({**m, "brought": brought})
    return out


def read_reverts(repo, since):
    fmt = SEP.join(["%H", "%at", "%ct", "%s", "%b"]) + "\x1e"
    reverts = []
    for rec in git(repo, "log", "--all", "-i", "--grep=^Revert", f"--since={since}", f"--format={fmt}").split("\x1e"):
        rec = rec.strip("\n")
        if not rec:
            continue
        sha, at, ct, subject, body = rec.split(SEP, 4)
        target_sha = re.search(r"reverts? commit ([0-9a-f]{7,40})", body, re.I)
        target_cl = re.search(r"reverts? CL (\d+)", body, re.I)
        reverts.append({
            "sha": sha, "at": int(at), "ct": int(ct), "subject": subject,
            "target_sha": target_sha.group(1) if target_sha else None,
            "target_cl": target_cl.group(1) if target_cl else None,
        })
    return reverts


def gerrit_change_map(repo, since, wanted):
    """Gerrit only: change number -> commit, from the Reviewed-on trailer."""
    out = git(repo, "log", "--all", f"--since={since}",
              "--format=%H %(trailers:key=Reviewed-on,valueonly,separator=%x20)")
    found = {}
    for line in out.splitlines():
        parts = line.split()
        if len(parts) >= 2:
            m = re.search(r"/\+/(\d+)", parts[1])
            if m and m.group(1) in wanted:
                found.setdefault(m.group(1), parts[0])
    return found


def main():
    start = iso_to_ts(CONFIG["window"]["start"])
    end = iso_to_ts(CONFIG["window"]["end"])
    OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    clones = json.loads((ROOT / "results" / "clones.json").read_text())
    for r in CONFIG["repos"]:
        repo = ROOT / "data" / "repos" / r["name"]
        branch = f"origin/{r['branch']}"
        tags = read_tags(repo, r["tag_pattern"])

        # A final release also gets the set measured against earlier final
        # releases only, because a release candidate is not a production deploy.
        deploys = []
        for i, t in enumerate(tags):
            if not (start <= t["ts"] < end):
                continue
            entry = {**t, "commits": shipped(repo, t, tags[:i])}
            if not t["prerelease"]:
                entry["commits_vs_finals"] = shipped(repo, t, [p for p in tags[:i] if not p["prerelease"]])
            deploys.append(entry)

        # Landings from a year before the window: stage 2 needs them to know when
        # a change shipped early in the window first landed.
        landings = landings_with_side_commits(repo, branch, start - YEAR, end)
        reverts = read_reverts(repo, start - YEAR)
        wanted = {rv["target_cl"] for rv in reverts if rv["target_cl"]}
        cl_map = gerrit_change_map(repo, start - 2 * YEAR, wanted) if wanted else {}

        extract = {
            "repo": r["name"],
            "merge_style": r["merge_style"],
            "head": clones[r["name"]]["head"],
            "window": CONFIG["window"],
            "code_hash": code_hash(),
            "generated_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
            "all_tags": tags,
            "deploys": deploys,
            "landings": landings,
            "reverts": reverts,
            "cl_map": cl_map,
        }
        path = OUT / f"{r['name']}.json.gz"
        with gzip.open(path, "wt") as f:
            json.dump(extract, f)
        in_window = sum(start <= m["ct"] < end for m in landings)
        print(f"{r['name']}: {len(deploys)} tags in window ({sum(not d['prerelease'] for d in deploys)} final), "
              f"{in_window} landings on {r['branch']}, {len(reverts)} reverts since a year before -> "
              f"{path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Stage 2: compute the four DORA metrics from results/extract/*.json, once per definition.

Each metric has several defensible definitions. This script computes all of them
side by side, so the spread is visible. Output: results/metrics.json.
"""
import gzip
import hashlib
import json
import math
import statistics
import sys
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
EXTRACT = ROOT / "results" / "extract"
HOUR = 3600
DAY = 86400
HOTFIX_DAYS = 7  # a release followed this soon by the next patch on the same line counts as failed


def iso_to_ts(value):
    return int(datetime.fromisoformat(value).timestamp())


def pct(values, q):
    """Nearest-rank percentile; values need not be sorted."""
    if not values:
        return None
    s = sorted(values)
    k = max(0, min(len(s) - 1, math.ceil(q / 100 * len(s)) - 1))
    return s[k]


def summary(values_seconds):
    if not values_seconds:
        return {"n": 0, "median_h": None, "p75_h": None}
    return {
        "n": len(values_seconds),
        "median_h": round(statistics.median(values_seconds) / HOUR, 2),
        "p75_h": round(pct(values_seconds, 75) / HOUR, 2),
    }


def metrics_for(ex):
    start = iso_to_ts(ex["window"]["start"])
    end = iso_to_ts(ex["window"]["end"])
    weeks = (end - start) / (7 * DAY)

    deploys = ex["deploys"]
    finals = [d for d in deploys if not d["prerelease"]]
    landings_all = ex["landings"]
    landings = [m for m in landings_all if start <= m["ct"] < end]

    # When each commit first landed on the default branch, and the landing it came with.
    landed = {}
    for m in landings_all:
        landed.setdefault(m["sha"], m)
        for c in m["brought"]:
            landed.setdefault(c["sha"], m)

    # ---- Deployment frequency -------------------------------------------------
    release_days = {datetime.fromtimestamp(d["ts"], timezone.utc).date() for d in finals}
    df = {
        "every_tag": len(deploys),
        "final_tags": len(finals),
        "release_days": len(release_days),
        "default_branch_landings": len(landings),
    }
    df = {k: {"count": v, "per_week": round(v / weeks, 4)} for k, v in df.items()}

    # ---- Change lead time (deploy = final release tag) ------------------------
    per_commit_author, per_commit_committer, per_commit_author_no_backports = [], [], []
    per_landing_from_landing, per_landing_from_first_commit = {}, {}
    backports = 0
    for d in finals:
        for c in d["commits_vs_finals"]:
            if len(c["parents"]) > 1:
                continue  # merge commits carry no change of their own
            per_commit_author.append(d["ts"] - c["at"])
            per_commit_committer.append(d["ts"] - c["ct"])
            m = landed.get(c["sha"])
            if m is not None:
                per_commit_author_no_backports.append(d["ts"] - c["at"])
            if m is None:
                # Never landed on the default branch: a backport or a release-branch commit.
                backports += 1
                key, land_ts, first_at = c["sha"], c["ct"], c["at"]
            else:
                key = m["sha"]
                land_ts = m["ct"]
                first_at = min([x["at"] for x in m["brought"]] or [m["at"]])
            # One value per landing (pull request or change), at its first release.
            if key not in per_landing_from_landing:
                per_landing_from_landing[key] = d["ts"] - land_ts
                per_landing_from_first_commit[key] = d["ts"] - first_at

    # Lead time if every tag counted, release candidates included: each commit at
    # the first tag that contained it.
    first_tag_author = [d["ts"] - c["at"] for d in deploys for c in d["commits"] if len(c["parents"]) == 1]

    # Lead time if every landing on the default branch counted as a deploy.
    landing_as_deploy, landing_merge, landing_single = [], [], []
    for m in landings:
        first_at = min([x["at"] for x in m["brought"]] or [m["at"]])
        landing_as_deploy.append(m["ct"] - first_at)
        (landing_merge if len(m["parents"]) > 1 else landing_single).append(m["ct"] - first_at)

    lt = {
        "commit_author_date_first_tag": summary(first_tag_author),
        "commit_author_date": summary(per_commit_author),
        "commit_committer_date": summary(per_commit_committer),
        "commit_author_date_without_backports": summary(per_commit_author_no_backports),
        "change_from_landing": summary(list(per_landing_from_landing.values())),
        "change_from_first_commit": summary(list(per_landing_from_first_commit.values())),
        "landing_is_deploy": summary(landing_as_deploy),
    }
    # The same measure split by how the change landed: a merge commit keeps the
    # side branch and its dates, a squash or rebase writes one new commit.
    landing_split = {
        "merge_commit_landings": summary(landing_merge),
        "single_commit_landings": summary(landing_single),
        # Squash and direct pushes: the author date is the landing time, to the minute.
        "single_commit_landings_author_within_60s_of_committer": sum(
            1 for m in landings if len(m["parents"]) == 1 and abs(m["ct"] - m["at"]) < 60),
    }

    # ---- Change failure rate and recovery (deploy = final release tag) --------
    # 1. Hotfix: the next release on the same major.minor line came within HOTFIX_DAYS.
    by_line = {}
    for t in ex["all_tags"]:
        if not t["prerelease"]:
            by_line.setdefault((t["major"], t["minor"]), []).append(t)
    hotfix_failed, hotfix_recovery = [], []
    for d in finals:
        line = sorted(by_line[(d["major"], d["minor"])], key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["patch"]))
        # Only fixes released inside the window, like the revert rule.
        later = [t for t in line if t["patch"] > d["patch"] and d["ts"] <= t["ts"] < end]
        if later and later[0]["ts"] - d["ts"] <= HOTFIX_DAYS * DAY:
            hotfix_failed.append(d["name"])
            hotfix_recovery.append(later[0]["ts"] - d["ts"])

    # 2. The release ships at least one revert commit.
    has_revert = [d["name"] for d in finals
                  if any(c["subject"].lower().startswith("revert") for c in d["commits_vs_finals"])]

    # 3. A later release reverts a change this release shipped.
    shipped_in = {}
    for d in sorted(finals, key=lambda x: x["ts"]):
        for c in d["commits_vs_finals"]:
            shipped_in.setdefault(c["sha"], d)
    revert_shas = {rv["sha"]: rv for rv in ex["reverts"]}

    def resolve(rv):
        if rv["target_sha"]:
            for sha in shipped_in:
                if sha.startswith(rv["target_sha"]):
                    return sha
            return None
        if rv["target_cl"]:
            return ex["cl_map"].get(rv["target_cl"])
        return None

    reverted_failed, reverted_recovery = {}, []
    unresolved_by_parser, target_not_in_earlier_release = 0, 0
    for d in finals:
        for c in d["commits_vs_finals"]:
            rv = revert_shas.get(c["sha"])
            if not rv or len(c["parents"]) > 1 or not c["subject"].lower().startswith("revert"):
                continue  # same candidates as rule 2: a non-merge commit whose subject starts with Revert
            if not rv["target_sha"] and not rv["target_cl"]:
                unresolved_by_parser += 1
                continue
            target = resolve(rv)
            if target is None or target not in shipped_in or shipped_in[target]["ts"] >= d["ts"]:
                # Reverted before it reached a release in the window, or shipped earlier.
                target_not_in_earlier_release += 1
                continue
            origin = shipped_in.get(target)
            if origin is None or origin["ts"] >= d["ts"] or origin["name"] in reverted_failed:
                continue
            reverted_failed[origin["name"]] = d["name"]
            reverted_recovery.append(d["ts"] - origin["ts"])

    n = len(finals)
    cfr = {
        "hotfix_within_7_days": {"failed": len(hotfix_failed), "of": n,
                                 "rate": round(len(hotfix_failed) / n, 3) if n else None},
        "ships_a_revert": {"failed": len(has_revert), "of": n,
                           "rate": round(len(has_revert) / n, 3) if n else None},
        "shipped_change_later_reverted": {"failed": len(reverted_failed), "of": n,
                                          "rate": round(len(reverted_failed) / n, 3) if n else None,
                                          "reverts_unresolved_by_parser": unresolved_by_parser,
                                          "reverts_of_unreleased_or_older_changes": target_not_in_earlier_release},
    }
    recovery = {
        "hotfix_within_7_days": summary(hotfix_recovery),
        "shipped_change_later_reverted": summary(reverted_recovery),
    }

    return {
        "repo": ex["repo"],
        "merge_style": ex["merge_style"],
        "head": ex["head"],
        "code_hash": ex["code_hash"],
        "weeks": round(weeks, 2),
        "deployment_frequency": df,
        "lead_time": lt,
        "landing_is_deploy_by_style": landing_split,
        "backport_commits": backports,
        "change_failure_rate": cfr,
        "recovery_time": recovery,
        "details": {
            "hotfix_failed": hotfix_failed,
            "ships_a_revert": has_revert,
            "reverted": reverted_failed,
        },
    }


def main():
    results = []
    hashes = set()
    for path in sorted(EXTRACT.glob("*.json.gz")):
        with gzip.open(path, "rt") as f:
            ex = json.load(f)
        hashes.add(ex["code_hash"])
        results.append(metrics_for(ex))
    if len(hashes) != 1:
        print(f"refusing: extracts come from different code versions {hashes}", file=sys.stderr)
        return 1
    out = {"generated_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
           "extract_code_hash": hashes.pop(),
           "metrics_code_hash": hashlib.sha256(Path(__file__).read_bytes()).hexdigest()[:16],
           "hotfix_days": HOTFIX_DAYS, "repos": results}
    (ROOT / "results" / "metrics.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2) + "\n")
    for r in results:
        print(f"== {r['repo']} ({r['merge_style']})")
        print("  deploys/week:", {k: v["per_week"] for k, v in r["deployment_frequency"].items()})
        print("  lead time median h:", {k: v["median_h"] for k, v in r["lead_time"].items()})
        print("  CFR:", {k: v["rate"] for k, v in r["change_failure_rate"].items()})
        print("  recovery median h:", {k: v["median_h"] for k, v in r["recovery_time"].items()})
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Stage 3: place each definition's result in the 2024 DORA performance levels.

The levels come from the 2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report (v. 2024.3,
page 13). They describe survey clusters, not official thresholds, so this is
the reading a dashboard would show, not a verdict on the projects.
Output: results/tiers.json and results/summary.md.
"""
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
WEEKS_PER_MONTH = 52.1775 / 12
WEEKS_PER_SIX_MONTHS = 52.1775 / 2

DF_LABELS = {
    "every_tag": "every tag (release candidates too)",
    "final_tags": "every final release tag",
    "release_days": "days with a final release",
    "default_branch_landings": "every landing on the default branch",
}
LT_LABELS = {
    "commit_author_date_first_tag": "commit author date to first tag (candidates too)",
    "commit_author_date": "commit author date to final release",
    "commit_committer_date": "commit committer date to final release",
    "commit_author_date_without_backports": "commit author date to final release, backports left out",
    "change_from_landing": "landing on default branch to final release (per change)",
    "change_from_first_commit": "first commit of the change to final release (per change)",
    "landing_is_deploy": "first commit to landing, if landing is the deploy (per change)",
}
CFR_LABELS = {
    "hotfix_within_7_days": "next patch on the same line within 7 days",
    "ships_a_revert": "release ships any revert commit",
    "shipped_change_later_reverted": "a later release reverts a change it shipped",
}


def df_level(per_week):
    if per_week > 7:
        return "Elite"
    if per_week >= 1:
        return "High"
    if per_week >= 1 / WEEKS_PER_MONTH:
        return "Medium"
    if per_week >= 1 / WEEKS_PER_SIX_MONTHS:
        return "Low"
    return "below Low"


def lt_level(hours):
    if hours is None:
        return "n/a"
    if hours < 24:
        return "Elite"
    if hours < 24 * 7:
        return "High"
    if hours < 24 * 30:
        return "Medium"
    if hours < 24 * 182.5:
        return "Low"
    return "below Low"


def fmt_hours(h):
    if h is None:
        return "n/a"
    if h < 48:
        return f"{h:.1f} h"
    return f"{h / 24:.1f} d"


def main():
    metrics = json.loads((ROOT / "results" / "metrics.json").read_text())
    out = {"source": "2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report, v. 2024.3, p. 13",
           "metrics_code_hash": metrics["metrics_code_hash"], "repos": []}
    md = ["# Results", "", f"Window: {metrics['repos'][0]['weeks']} weeks. "
          f"Levels: 2024 DORA report, p. 13. Code: extract {metrics['extract_code_hash']}, "
          f"metrics {metrics['metrics_code_hash']}.", ""]
    for r in metrics["repos"]:
        rec = {"repo": r["repo"], "merge_style": r["merge_style"], "df": {}, "lt": {}}
        md += [f"## {r['repo']} ({r['merge_style']}, head {r['head'][:12]})", "",
               "| Deploy means | Deploys | Per week | Level |", "| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |"]
        for k, v in r["deployment_frequency"].items():
            lvl = df_level(v["per_week"])
            rec["df"][k] = {**v, "level": lvl}
            md.append(f"| {DF_LABELS[k]} | {v['count']} | {v['per_week']:.2f} | {lvl} |")
        md += ["", "| Lead time measured from | Changes | Median | p75 | Level (median) |",
               "| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |"]
        for k, v in r["lead_time"].items():
            lvl = lt_level(v["median_h"])
            rec["lt"][k] = {**v, "level": lvl}
            md.append(f"| {LT_LABELS[k]} | {v['n']} | {fmt_hours(v['median_h'])} | {fmt_hours(v['p75_h'])} | {lvl} |")
        md += ["", "| A release failed if | Failed | Of | Rate |", "| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |"]
        for k, v in r["change_failure_rate"].items():
            md.append(f"| {CFR_LABELS[k]} | {v['failed']} | {v['of']} | {v['rate'] * 100:.1f}% |")
        rec["cfr"] = r["change_failure_rate"]
        rec["recovery"] = r["recovery_time"]
        md += ["", "Recovery time (median): " + ", ".join(
            f"{CFR_LABELS[k]}: {fmt_hours(v['median_h'])} (n={v['n']})" for k, v in r["recovery_time"].items()), ""]
        df_levels = sorted({v["level"] for v in rec["df"].values()})
        lt_levels = sorted({v["level"] for v in rec["lt"].values()})
        rec["df_levels_reached"] = df_levels
        rec["lt_levels_reached"] = lt_levels
        md += [f"Deployment frequency levels reached: {', '.join(df_levels)}. "
               f"Lead time levels reached: {', '.join(lt_levels)}.", ""]
        out["repos"].append(rec)
    (ROOT / "results" / "tiers.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2) + "\n")
    (ROOT / "results" / "summary.md").write_text("\n".join(md) + "\n")
    print("\n".join(md))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Cross-check stage 1 against plain git on random samples (seeded), for every repo in repos.json.

1. The side commits a merge brought in must equal
   `git rev-list --no-merges <first parent>..<merge>`.
2. The first final release that ships a commit must equal the earliest final
   tag that `git tag --contains` lists for it.
Output: results/method-checks.txt. Exits 1 on any mismatch.
"""
import gzip
import json
import random
import re
import subprocess
import sys
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
SEED = 7


def git(repo, *args):
    return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(ROOT / "data" / "repos" / repo), *args],
                          check=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout


def load(repo):
    with gzip.open(ROOT / "results" / "extract" / f"{repo}.json.gz", "rt") as f:
        return json.load(f)


def main():
    rng = random.Random(SEED)
    config = json.loads((ROOT / "repos.json").read_text())
    start = int(datetime.fromisoformat(config["window"]["start"]).timestamp())
    lines, failures = [], 0
    for repo in [r["name"] for r in config["repos"]]:
        ex = load(repo)
        merges = [m for m in ex["landings"] if len(m["parents"]) > 1 and m["ct"] >= start]
        sample = rng.sample(merges, min(25, len(merges)))
        ok = 0
        for m in sample:
            want = set(git(repo, "rev-list", "--no-merges", f"{m['parents'][0]}..{m['sha']}").split())
            ok += want == {c["sha"] for c in m["brought"]}
        failures += len(sample) - ok
        if sample:
            lines.append(f"{repo}: side commits match git rev-list for {ok} of {len(sample)} random merges")

        finals = {t["name"]: t for t in ex["all_tags"] if not t["prerelease"]}
        pairs = [(c["sha"], d["name"]) for d in ex["deploys"] if not d["prerelease"]
                 for c in d["commits_vs_finals"] if len(c["parents"]) == 1]
        sample = rng.sample(pairs, min(15, len(pairs)))
        ok = 0
        for sha, name in sample:
            tags = [finals[t] for t in git(repo, "tag", "--contains", sha).split() if t in finals]
            first = min(tags, key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["name"]))["name"] if tags else None
            ok += first == name
        failures += len(sample) - ok
        lines.append(f"{repo}: first final release matches git tag --contains for {ok} of {len(sample)} random commits")
    text = "\n".join(lines) + "\n"
    (ROOT / "results" / "method-checks.txt").write_text(text)
    print(text, end="")
    return 1 if failures else 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

# Record what a full run costs on this machine: hardware, wall time, clone size.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
{
  echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
  echo "machine: $(tr -d '\0' < /proc/device-tree/model 2>/dev/null || uname -m)"
  echo "kernel: $(uname -sr)"
  echo "git: $(git --version)"
  echo "python: $(python3 --version)"
  s=$(date +%s.%N)
  ./run.sh > /dev/null
  e=$(date +%s.%N)
  printf 'run.sh wall time: %.1f s\n' "$(echo "$e - $s" | bc -l)"
  echo "clone sizes:"
  du -sh data/repos/* | sed 's/^/  /'
  echo "  total: $(du -sh data/repos | cut -f1)"
} | tee results/benchmark.txt

     1	---
     2	title: 'DORA Metrics From Git History: The Definition Picks Your Tier'
     3	excerpt: 'We computed deployment frequency, lead time, change failure rate and recovery time from one year of Kubernetes, uv, Go and Prometheus history, once per common definition. The definition alone moved every project across two or three DORA performance levels, and the merge button decides whether Git still knows when the work started.'

     6	  slug: 'devops'
     7	date: '2026-10-14'

     9	updatedAt: '2026-10-14T09:00:00Z'
    10	readingTime: '14 min read'
    11	author:
    12	  name: 'DevOps Daily Team'
    13	  slug: 'devops-daily-team'

    17	  - Engineering Metrics
