    21	  - DevOps
    22	---
    23	
    24	Most DORA dashboards start from the same raw material: your Git history, your tags and your pipeline events. We wanted to know how much of the final number comes from that data and how much comes from the definitions the tool picks for you. So we computed the four DORA metrics for one year of four open-source projects, under each definition we see in scripts and tools, and placed every result in the performance levels of the 2024 DORA report.
    25	
    26	The definition alone moved every project across at least two levels. From the same Kubernetes history, deployment frequency is **Medium** (42 final releases, 0.81 a week), **High** (63 tags with the alpha, beta and rc tags, 1.21 a week) or **Elite** (3,286 landings on master, 63 a week). Lead time is **High** (5.2 days) if a landing on master counts as the deploy, **Medium** (25.9 days) to the first tag, and **Low** (76.6 days) to the first final release. For uv, which squash-merges, the lead time from first commit to landing is 0.0 hours, because its squash commits keep no record of when the work started.
    27	
    28	## TL;DR
    29	
    30	- **A deploy is a definition.** Every project lands in **Elite** for deployment frequency if each landing on the default branch counts, and in **Medium** or **High** if releases count.
    31	- **Lead time depends on where the clock starts and stops.** Kubernetes ranges from 5.2 days to 81.9 days on the same history, depending on the start timestamp, the event that stops the clock, and whether backports count.
    32	- **The merge button can erase history.** In 3,090 of uv's 3,132 landings, the author date is within a minute of the landing time. Prometheus uses both buttons: changes merged with a merge commit show 48.2 hours from first commit to merge, squashed changes show 0.0 hours.
    33	- **Change failure rate is the least stable of the four.** uv is at 86.1%, 19.8% or 5.0% depending on whether a failure is a quick patch release, any revert, or a revert of a change that shipped.
    34	- **What to do:** write the definitions down, keep them fixed, and compare a team with its own past, not with a level chart or another tool.
    35	
    36	## Prerequisites
    37	
    38	- `git` and `python3` (standard library only), plus `bc` for the merge-style demo
    39	- A repository whose tags mark releases with version numbers
    40	- The companion repo [dora-from-git](https://github.com/The-DevOps-Daily/dora-from-git), which holds every script and every recorded result in this post
    41	
    42	## How we measured
    43	
    44	We took one year, 1 October 2025 to 1 October 2026, and four projects that merge in different ways:
    45	
    46	| Repo                            | How changes land                                                    | What marks a release                        |
    47	| ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
    48	| kubernetes/kubernetes           | merge commits                                                       | `vX.Y.Z` tags, plus alpha, beta and rc tags |
    49	| astral-sh/uv                    | squash                                                              | `X.Y.Z` tags                                |
    50	| golang/go (go.googlesource.com) | rebase through Gerrit, almost linear history                        | `goX.Y.Z` tags, plus beta and rc tags       |
    51	| prometheus/prometheus           | mixed: 626 merge commits and 615 squashed or pushed commits on main | `vX.Y.Z` tags, plus rc tags                 |
    52	
    53	The scripts use plain Git and nothing else. Go is hosted on Gerrit, not GitHub, and the same code reads it. Treeless clones keep every commit and tag but no file contents, which is enough for metadata: the four repos take 132 MB, and a full run took 137 seconds on a Raspberry Pi 4 (`results/benchmark.txt`).
    54	
    55	The pipeline is four stages, each writing a file the next one reads:
    56	
    57	1. `00-clone.sh` clones each repo and records its head commit.
    58	2. `01-extract.py` records, for every tag in the window, the commits it shipped for the first time, every first-parent commit on the default branch with the commits it brought in, and every revert with the commit or Gerrit change its message names.
    59	3. `02-metrics.py` computes each metric under each definition.
    60	4. `03-tiers.py` places the results in the 2024 levels.
    61	
    62	Before we trusted the extract, `check-method.py` compared it with plain Git on seeded random samples. The commits each merge brought in matched `git rev-list --no-merges <first-parent>..<merge>` on 58 of 58 merges, and the first final release that shipped a commit matched `git tag --contains` on 60 of 60 commits.
    63	
    64	The levels come from the [2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report](https://dora.dev/research/2024/dora-report/) (version 2024.3, page 13):
    65	
    66	| Level  | Change lead time                 | Deployment frequency                             | Change fail rate | Failed deployment recovery time |
    67	| ------ | -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ---------------- | ------------------------------- |
    68	| Elite  | Less than one day                | On demand (multiple deploys per day)             | 5%               | Less than one hour              |
    69	| High   | Between one day and one week     | Between once per day and once per week           | 20%              | Less than one day               |
    70	| Medium | Between one week and one month   | Between once per week and once per month         | 10%              | Less than one day               |
    71	| Low    | Between one month and six months | Between once per month and once every six months | 40%              | Between one week and one month  |
    72	
    73	The report describes these as clusters of survey answers, not as thresholds. We use the ranges the way a dashboard does, as bands. We place only deployment frequency and lead time, because change fail rate is a single value per level and is not in order: Medium (10%) sits below High (20%).
    74	
    75	:::note
    76	These projects ship releases, not production deploys, so a tag stands in for a deploy. That makes them a fair test of the definitions, and an unfair basis for ranking the projects. Nothing here says one project is better run than another.
    77	:::
    78	
    79	## What counts as a deploy
    80	
    81	Four common definitions: every tag, every final release, every day with a final release, and every landing on the default branch (a merge, a squash or a direct commit). A tool that reads only the Git host sees no deploys, so the last one is the event it can count.
    82	
    83	| Repo       | Every tag         | Final releases    | Release days      | Landings on default branch |
    84	| ---------- | ----------------- | ----------------- | ----------------- | -------------------------- |
    85	| Kubernetes | 1.21/week, High   | 0.81/week, Medium | 0.29/week, Medium | 63.0/week, Elite           |
    86	| uv         | 1.94/week, High   | 1.94/week, High   | 1.78/week, High   | 60.1/week, Elite           |
    87	| Go         | 0.69/week, Medium | 0.58/week, Medium | 0.29/week, Medium | 65.6/week, Elite           |
    88	| Prometheus | 0.75/week, Medium | 0.50/week, Medium | 0.46/week, Medium | 23.8/week, Elite           |
    89	
    90	Kubernetes shows how much the choice matters. It cut 42 final releases in the year, but only on 15 days, because it patches several release lines on the same day. Count tags, releases or release days and you get three different numbers from the same calendar. Count landings and every project is Elite, including Go, whose master branch is not a release.
    91	
    92	Your company has the same choice, with different names: every merge to main, every pipeline run, every rollout to production, or every release train. Pick one and write it down.
    93	
    94	## Where the lead time clock starts and stops
    95	
    96	Lead time has two ends, and Git offers several timestamps for each.
    97	
    98	```diagram
    99	{
   100	  "type": "flow",
   101	  "nodes": [
   102	    { "label": "First commit", "sub": "author date", "icon": "branch", "tone": "slate" },
   103	    { "label": "Last rebase or amend", "sub": "committer date", "icon": "gear", "tone": "slate" },
   104	    { "label": "Lands on main", "sub": "merge or submit", "icon": "check", "tone": "blue" },
   105	    { "label": "First tag", "sub": "alpha, beta or rc", "icon": "box", "tone": "amber" },
   106	    { "label": "Final release", "sub": "the deploy", "icon": "rocket", "tone": "green" }
   107	  ]
   108	}
   109	```
   110	
   111	Here is Kubernetes, from the same history, seven ways. The rows do not cover identical sets of changes: a landing-based row counts what landed in the window, a release-based row counts what releases in the window shipped, and each stop event picks up a slightly different set.
   112	
   113	| Clock starts                           | Clock stops                              | Changes | Median | Level  |
   114	| -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ------: | -----: | ------ |
   115	| first commit of the change             | landing on master, if that is the deploy |   3,286 |  5.2 d | High   |
   116	| commit author date                     | first tag, alpha to rc included          |   6,812 | 25.9 d | Medium |
   117	| landing on master                      | first final release                      |   3,734 | 60.6 d | Low    |
   118	| commit committer date                  | first final release                      |   6,091 | 63.1 d | Low    |
   119	| commit author date                     | first final release                      |   6,091 | 76.6 d | Low    |
   120	| first commit of the change             | first final release                      |   3,734 | 77.0 d | Low    |
   121	| commit author date, backports left out | first final release                      |   5,486 | 81.9 d | Low    |
   122	
   123	```chart
   124	{
   125	  "type": "bar",
   126	  "title": "Kubernetes change lead time, same history, seven definitions",
   127	  "unit": " days",
   128	  "caption": "Median days. 1 Oct 2025 to 1 Oct 2026. Source: results/summary.md in The-DevOps-Daily/dora-from-git.",
   129	  "rows": [
   130	    { "label": "Landing is the deploy", "value": 5.2, "series": "High" },
   131	    { "label": "To first tag (alpha to rc)", "value": 25.9, "series": "Medium" },
   132	    { "label": "Landing to final release", "value": 60.6, "series": "Low" },
   133	    { "label": "Committer date to final", "value": 63.1, "series": "Low" },
   134	    { "label": "Author date to final", "value": 76.6, "series": "Low" },
   135	    { "label": "First commit of change to final", "value": 77.0, "series": "Low" },
   136	    { "label": "Author date, no backports", "value": 81.9, "series": "Low" }
   137	  ],
   138	  "series": [
   139	    { "name": "High", "color": "#10b981" },
   140	    { "name": "Medium", "color": "#f59e0b" },
   141	    { "name": "Low", "color": "#ef4444" }
   142	  ]
   143	}
   144	```
   145	
   146	Four choices do most of the moving:
   147	
   148	- **Which release stops the clock.** Kubernetes cuts alpha tags from master, so a commit reaches its first tag 25.9 days after it is written, and its first final release 76.6 days after. If your release candidates never serve users, they are not deploys.
   149	- **Author date or committer date.** The committer date changes every time a commit is rebased or amended, the author date does not. On Kubernetes, switching to the committer date moves the median from 76.6 to 63.1 days.
   150	- **Backports.** For 605 of the 6,091 commits that Kubernetes final releases shipped, we found no landing on master between October 2024 and October 2026. They were made on release branches: mostly cherry-picked fixes, plus the release commits themselves. `git cherry-pick` keeps the original author date, so a cherry-pick counts the time since the fix was first written. Leaving these commits out moves the median from 76.6 to 81.9 days.
   151	- **Per commit or per change.** We count a change as one landing on the default branch, so a pull request merged with a merge commit counts once, while a rebased pull request with ten commits counts ten times, and a commit with no landing on master counts on its own, from its commit date. On Kubernetes, per commit and per change barely differ (76.6 against 77.0 days). The unit matters more where pull requests are large.
   152	
   153	Go is Low in every release-based definition, from 69.1 days (to the first tag, beta and rc included) to 138.0 days (to the final release, backports left out). It ships a feature release every six months, and in our window go1.26.0 was tagged on 10 February 2026 and go1.27.0 on 19 August 2026. Count a landing on master as the deploy and the same project is High, at 4.1 days.
   154	
   155	The other repos show smaller spreads. Prometheus is Low (31.9 to 40.5 days) in every release-based definition. uv releases about twice a week, so it is High (28.6 to 30.1 hours) in all of them.
   156	
   157	## The merge button decides what Git remembers
   158	
   159	This is the part we did not expect to be so clean. With the default behaviour of each button, the way a change lands rewrites its timestamps, and the lead time follows. `demo-date-rewrite.sh` writes two commits on Monday and Wednesday, lands them on Friday three ways, and reads the result back:
   160	
   161	```terminal
   162	{
   163	  "title": "one change, three merge buttons",
   164	  "prompt": "$",
   165	  "autoplay": false,
   166	  "steps": [
   167	    { "cmd": "./scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh", "output": "merge commit:\n  feature: part 2 | author 2026-09-09T15:00:00+00:00 | committer 2026-09-09T15:00:00+00:00\n  feature: part 1 | author 2026-09-07T10:00:00+00:00 | committer 2026-09-07T10:00:00+00:00\n  lead time from author date 102.0 h, from committer date 102.0 h\n\nsquash:\n  feature (squashed) | author 2026-09-11T16:00:00+00:00 | committer 2026-09-11T16:00:00+00:00\n  lead time from author date   0.0 h, from committer date   0.0 h\n\nrebase:\n  feature: part 2 | author 2026-09-09T15:00:00+00:00 | committer 2026-09-11T16:00:00+00:00\n  feature: part 1 | author 2026-09-07T10:00:00+00:00 | committer 2026-09-11T16:00:00+00:00\n  lead time from author date 102.0 h, from committer date   0.0 h" }
   168	  ]
   169	}
   170	```
   171	
   172	A merge commit keeps the original commits and their dates. A squash writes one new commit dated at the landing. A rebase keeps the author dates but resets the committer dates. Git lets you override both dates, so these are defaults, not laws, and a commit date is not proof of when a commit was pushed.
   173	
   174	The real repos behave the same way:
   175	
   176	- **uv** lands every change as a single commit. In 3,090 of its 3,132 landings, the author date is within 60 seconds of the commit date, so "first commit to landing" is 0.0 hours at the median and at the 75th percentile. Git cannot tell how long a uv pull request was open.
   177	- **Prometheus** uses both buttons. The 626 changes it landed with a merge commit took 48.2 hours from first commit to merge at the median. The 615 squashed or pushed ones show 0.0 hours, and in 612 of them the author date is within a minute of the commit date. Same project, same maintainers, same year.
   178	- **Go** submits through Gerrit, which rebases but keeps the author date. None of its 3,414 single-commit landings has an author date within 60 seconds of the commit date, and the median from author date to submit is 99.0 hours (4.1 days).
   179	
   180	So with these defaults, a team that squash-merges looks Elite on "first commit to landing" lead time however long its reviews take, and a team that switches from merge commits to squash improves that number overnight without changing anything else.
   181	
   182	## Three rules for a failed deploy
   183	
   184	Git has no incidents, so a Git-only tool has to infer failures. We tested three common rules:
   185	
   186	| Repo       | Next patch within 7 days | Release ships any revert | A later release reverts a change it shipped |
   187	| ---------- | ------------------------ | ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |
   188	| Kubernetes | 0 of 42 (0.0%)           | 4 of 42 (9.5%)           | 1 of 42 (2.4%)                              |
   189	| uv         | 87 of 101 (86.1%)        | 20 of 101 (19.8%)        | 5 of 101 (5.0%)                             |
   190	| Go         | 4 of 30 (13.3%)          | 2 of 30 (6.7%)           | 1 of 30 (3.3%)                              |
   191	| Prometheus | 5 of 26 (19.2%)          | 7 of 26 (26.9%)          | 1 of 26 (3.8%)                              |
   192	
   193	Each rule fails in its own way:
   194	
   195	- **The hotfix rule reads a version scheme as failures.** uv increments the patch number for nearly every release and shipped 101 in the year, so the next patch almost always follows within a week: 86.1%. Kubernetes publishes patch releases monthly, so the same rule never fires: 0.0%. Neither number says anything about failures.
   196	- **"Ships a revert" counts reverts that users never saw.** Many reverts undo a change before it reaches a release. In Kubernetes, 34 reverts that shipped in a final release undid a change that had not shipped in an earlier release in the window: either it was never released, or it shipped before the window.
   197	- **"Reverts a shipped change" needs the revert to name its target in a form a script can read.** Our parser looks for "reverts commit <hash>" and "reverts CL <number>". It could not resolve 21 Kubernetes reverts and 19 uv reverts: their messages name no commit that way, sometimes none at all, so Git alone cannot tell what they undid. Go writes "This reverts CL 847405" instead of a commit hash, and the script resolves those through the `Reviewed-on` trailer that Gerrit adds. Your conventions decide what this rule can see.
   198	
   199	The strict rule finds one failure each in Kubernetes, Go and Prometheus. With one event, the rate is noise: one more revert would double it.
   200	
   201	Recovery time inherits all of this. Under the hotfix rule it is the time to the next patch: 3.0 days for uv, 5.2 for Prometheus, 6.0 for Go. It can never be longer than 7 days, because the rule only counts a failure when the fix came within 7 days. The number measures the rule, not the recovery.
   202	
   203	## What a dashboard adds, and what it does not
   204	
   205	The script shows what Git alone supports. Engineering-metrics tools such as LinearB, Swarmia, Jellyfish, DX, Sleuth and Faros AI connect to more systems than Git, and those systems hold data Git does not:
   206	
   207	- **When the work started.** The forge API knows when a pull request was opened and when its first commit was pushed, which is exactly what a squash merge removes from Git.
   208	- **Real deploy events.** A CD pipeline knows when a version reached production, per environment. A tag is a stand-in.
   209	- **Real failures.** Linking deploys to incidents, rollbacks and pages measures failure directly instead of inferring it from reverts or patch numbers.
   210	- **Ownership and trends.** Mapping repos and paths to services and teams, and recomputing the same way every week.
   211	
   212	What a tool does not remove is the choice of definition. Before you compare a number across teams or tools, ask which event counts as a deploy, which timestamp starts lead time, whether backports and release candidates count, and what counts as a failure. If the answers differ, the numbers do not compare.
   213	
   214	## What we could not conclude
   215	
   216	- **These projects do not deploy.** Tags stand in for deploys. The results test the definitions, not the projects.
   217	- **The levels are survey clusters.** The report presents them as groups of answers, and the band edges are our reading.
   218	- **The failure numbers are small.** The strict revert rule found one failure in three of the four projects, so its rates move a lot with one release.
   219	- **Unshipped work is left out.** Lead time counts only commits that reached a release before the window closed, so the medians describe completed work. Go's work since the go1.27 freeze is not in them.
   220	- **Tag dates differ.** uv and Go use lightweight tags, dated by the tagged commit. Kubernetes and Prometheus use annotated tags, dated by the tagger. A release can be published hours after its tag.
   221	
   222	## Run it on your repositories
   223	
   224	```bash
   225	git clone https://github.com/The-DevOps-Daily/dora-from-git.git
   226	cd dora-from-git
   227	./run.sh
   228	cat results/summary.md
   229	```
   230	
   231	To measure your own repositories, edit `repos.json`. Each entry needs a clone URL, the default branch, and a regular expression for your release tags, with groups for major, minor and patch and an optional fourth group for prerelease suffixes:
   232	
   233	```json
   234	{
   235	  "name": "payments-api",
   236	  "url": "git@gitlab.example.com:platform/payments-api.git",
   237	  "branch": "main",
   238	  "merge_style": "squash",
   239	  "tag_pattern": "^v(\\d+)\\.(\\d+)\\.(\\d+)(-rc\\.\\d+)?$"
   240	}
   241	```
   242	
   243	If your deploys are not tagged, have your pipeline tag each production deploy with a version that the pattern matches, for example `v1.42.0`, and the same scripts measure deploys instead of releases. The hotfix rule reads the patch number, so it only means something if a patch bump marks a fix.
   244	
   245	```github
   246	https://github.com/The-DevOps-Daily/dora-from-git
   247	```
   248	
   249	## Summary
   250	
   251	- The same Git history put each of the four projects in two or three different DORA levels, depending only on the definition.
   252	- What counts as a deploy moved deployment frequency by up to two levels, and every project is Elite if a landing on the default branch counts.
   253	- Where the clock starts and stops moved Kubernetes lead time from 5.2 to 81.9 days on the same history.
   254	- With default settings, squash merges and rebases rewrite the timestamps lead time depends on, so the merge button can change the metric without changing the work.
   255	- Change failure rate inferred from Git measured version schemes and commit message habits more than failures.
   256	- Write the definitions down, keep them fixed, record the unit (commit or change), and compare a team with its own past.

The post mostly reproduces the saved numbers correctly, but its central comparison changes populations as well as definitions. There are also concrete problems in failure detection and the instructions for using other repositories.

Below, `post` refers to [dora-metrics-from-git-history.md](/tmp/claude-1000/-home-biliev-projects-devops-daily/f84689a1-064c-4f2a-9937-e3b6665620ab/scratchpad/wt-dora/content/posts/dora-metrics-from-git-history.md).

1. **High — “Same commits, seven definitions” is not a like-for-like comparison.**  
   **Locations:** `post:111–126`, `post:148`, `post:253`; [02-metrics.py:76](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:76), [02-metrics.py:100](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:100).  
   Kubernetes’s rows contain **3,286 landings**, **6,812 first-tag commits**, **6,091 first-final commits**, **3,734 grouped changes**, and **5,486 commits after filtering**. Landings are selected by landing date; release rows are selected by release date and can include earlier landings. Changing the stopping event also changes which observations enter the sample. Consequently, **5.2 → 81.9 days** does not isolate clock-definition effects on identical work. Say “same repository history, different definitions and cohorts,” or use a matched cohort. The **76.6 versus 63.1 days** author/committer comparison does use the same commit population.

2. **Medium — Failure rules use different follow-up windows, affecting a headline percentage.**  
   **Locations:** `post:186–191`; [02-metrics.py:130](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:130), [02-metrics.py:148](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:148), [metrics.json:571](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/metrics.json:571).  
   Hotfix detection searches **all tags**, including those after October 1; strict revert detection searches only releases inside the window. The saved uv extract shows `0.12.21` on September 29 counted as failed because `0.12.22` appeared on **October 1 at 23:31 UTC**, outside the window. Restricting this hotfix comparison to in-window fixes gives **87/101 = 86.1%**, rather than **88/101 = 87.1%**. Following failures beyond the window is defensible, but the rules need a consistent, disclosed follow-up policy. Strict revert rates and recovery times also omit unresolved outcomes at the window boundary.

3. **Medium — “Landing on master” includes commits that have no recorded master landing; “per change” does not consistently mean per PR.**  
   **Locations:** `post:117`, `post:120`, `post:150–151`; [02-metrics.py:82](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:82), [README.md:36](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/README.md:36).  
   When a commit lacks a landing lookup, the code still includes it in both per-change metrics, substituting its **committer date** and counting its SHA as a separate change. Kubernetes’s 3,734 observations therefore include the **605 unmatched commits**. The 60.6-day row is a mixture of master-landing and commit-date clocks. Additionally, a rebased ten-commit PR becomes ten first-parent observations, not one change. Describe the grouping precisely. “Never landed on master” also exceeds what the bounded landing lookup proves.

4. **Medium — “25 Kubernetes reverts and 22 uv reverts name no commit” misstates what the parser measured.**  
   **Locations:** `post:197`; [01-extract.py:107](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/01-extract.py:107), [02-metrics.py:170](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:170).  
   These counters mean **neither of two case-sensitive body patterns matched**, not that no target was named. Concrete examples in the saved history:
   - Kubernetes `3ec711f91…` explicitly names commit `6031ff29c144fe16e98f6fcb1d7201634f9a173e`.
   - uv `442a3bfd…` explicitly says to revert handling from `17b195bd714`.

   Both are counted as naming no commit. Furthermore, `--grep=^Revert` searches message-body lines too: Kubernetes `5158bdaac…` matches prose about a transaction being “reverted,” rather than a Git revert. This differs from rule two’s subject-prefix filter. Rename the counter to “unresolved by this parser,” and align the candidate rules. [Git’s documentation confirms that `--grep` searches the log message.](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-log)

5. **Medium — The merge-button conclusion treats configurable timestamps as guaranteed landing events.**  
   **Locations:** `post:172`, `post:180`; [demo-date-rewrite.sh:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh:10), [demo-date-rewrite.sh:50](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh:50), [02-metrics.py:105](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:105).  
   The demo explicitly sets both squash timestamps to Friday and the rebase committer timestamp to Friday. The metric then uses the committer timestamp as “landing.” This demonstrates that workflow, but does not establish that every squash-merging team necessarily looks Elite. Commit timestamps also do not independently record when an existing commit was pushed or fast-forwarded onto the remote branch. Retain the observed uv statistics; qualify the universal claim. Git permits overriding both author-date and committer-date behavior. [Git rebase documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rebase)

6. **Medium — The advertised custom-repository workflow does not work as written.**  
   **Locations:** `post:231–243`; [check-method.py:36](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:36), [check-method.py:49](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:49), [run.sh:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/run.sh:10).  
   Replacing `repos.json` with another repository leaves the mandatory checker hard-coded to Kubernetes, Prometheus, Go, and uv. A fresh custom run fails loading their extracts; an existing checkout checks unrelated old extracts. Separately, `deploy-2026-10-14-1` matches none of the supplied release patterns. The extractor requires numeric version groups plus a fourth capture group, and hotfix detection depends on version ordering. Arbitrary deploy tags require configuration and metric changes, not just the suggested `git tag` command.

7. **Medium — Several factual claims lack recorded evidence.**  
   **Locations:** `post:53`, `post:81`, `post:184`, `post:205–210`; [README.md:7](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/README.md:7).  
   **134 MB**, **56 seconds**, and the **Raspberry Pi 5** benchmark are repeated in the README but have no measurement log or hardware record in the reviewed evidence. Nor do the files establish that these are definitions “all in real use,” the “three rules we see most often,” or capabilities shared by every named vendor. Record the benchmark conditions and cite implementations/product documentation, or present these as illustrative definitions and possible integrations.

8. **Medium — The direction of the unshipped-work bias is asserted without evidence.**  
   **Location:** `post:219`.  
   Excluding unfinished work creates censoring, but does not establish that Go’s observed median is necessarily pulled downward. Recently started, unshipped changes could eventually finish faster than the current median. The files provide neither a counterfactual completed cohort nor a censoring analysis. State that the median describes completed releases and excludes unfinished work.

9. **Low — The extractor misassigns history at its lookback boundary.**  
   **Locations:** [01-extract.py:82](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/01-extract.py:82), [check-method.py:41](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/check-method.py:41).  
   `assigned` starts empty, and traversal stops only at first-parent commits inside the lookback slice. It can therefore absorb ancestors already present before that slice. The saved Kubernetes extract assigns **4,063 brought commits** to merge `35065cb…`; reading its actual first-parent range shows **one**, `6933c1bc2`. The random checks exclude this warm-up period. This can corrupt landing attribution for old work released later. **I found no overlap between those 4,063 commits and this window’s first-final shipments**, so this example does not establish a changed current headline.

10. **Low — Go’s final-release frequency rounds incorrectly.**  
    **Locations:** `post:87`; [summary.md:10](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/summary.md:10), [02-metrics.py:70](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:70).  
    `30 ÷ (365/7) = 0.575342…`, which rounds to **0.58/week**, not **0.57/week**. Rounding to three decimals before formatting to two produces the discrepancy. The Medium classification is unaffected.

11. **Low — Several labels are more specific than the evidence supports.**  
    **Locations:** `post:26`, `post:50`, `post:153`, `post:195`; [metrics.json:190](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/metrics.json:190), [metrics.json:68](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/results/metrics.json:68).  
    Kubernetes’s **3,286** observations are **3,135 merge commits plus 151 single-parent landings**. Go also has **eight merge landings**, so its history is not entirely linear. Go’s **69.1 days** is time to the first matching tag, including beta/final tags—not specifically the first rc. And uv does not increment the patch number for *every* release: the results include `0.9.0`, `0.10.0`, and other minor-version transitions.

12. **Low — The percentile helper is not nearest-rank as documented.**  
    **Location:** [02-metrics.py:26](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:26).  
    It uses `round(q/100*n + 0.5)` instead of `ceil(q/100*n)`. For four observations, p75 selects the fourth value instead of the third. This affects saved percentiles, including Go’s four-event hotfix recovery summary; it does not affect medians or median-based tiers.

The remaining displayed counts, percentages, median conversions, demo’s **102 hours**, and recorded **50/50** and **45/45** checks match the evidence. The Go release dates and tag-type claims also match the saved extracts. The extraction and metrics code hashes match the recorded hashes.

The **DORA table is quoted correctly**, including High’s 20% versus Medium’s 10% failure rate. All displayed tiers follow [03-tiers.py:39](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/03-tiers.py:39). Its weekly-average thresholds and 30-/182.5-day boundaries are custom operational interpretations, as the post acknowledges; the supplied report does not prescribe those numerical cutoffs.

     1	# dora-from-git

     3	```bash

     6	
     7	Compute the four DORA metrics (deployment frequency, change lead time, change failure rate, recovery time) from plain Git history, once per definition, and see how far the definition alone moves the result. It needs `git`, `python3` (standard library only) and `bc`. `scripts/benchmark.sh` records what a run costs: 137 seconds on a Raspberry Pi 4 Model B, with 132 MB of treeless clones (`results/benchmark.txt`).
     8	
     9	The repos it measures, one per merge style, over 1 October 2025 to 1 October 2026:
    10	
    11	| Repo | Merge style | Deploy record |
    12	| --- | --- | --- |
    13	| kubernetes/kubernetes | merge commits | `vX.Y.Z` tags, alpha, beta and rc tags |
    14	| astral-sh/uv | squash | `X.Y.Z` tags |
    15	| golang/go (go.googlesource.com) | rebase through Gerrit, almost linear history | `goX.Y.Z` tags, beta and rc tags |
    16	| prometheus/prometheus | mixed: about half merge commits, half squash | `vX.Y.Z` tags and rc tags |
    17	
    18	Edit `repos.json` to point it at your own repositories. Any Git host works: nothing here calls a forge API.
    19	
    20	## What each script does
    21	
    22	| Script | Reads | Writes |
    23	| --- | --- | --- |
    24	| `scripts/00-clone.sh` | `repos.json` | treeless clones in `data/repos/` (commits and tags, no file contents), `results/clones.json` with the head of each |
    25	| `scripts/01-extract.py` | the clones | `results/extract/<repo>.json.gz`: tags and the commits each one shipped, every first-parent commit on the default branch with the commits it brought in, and every revert with the commit or Gerrit change it names |
    26	| `scripts/02-metrics.py` | the extracts | `results/metrics.json`: every metric under every definition |
    27	| `scripts/03-tiers.py` | `results/metrics.json` | `results/tiers.json` and `results/summary.md`: each result placed in the 2024 DORA performance levels |
    28	| `scripts/demo-date-rewrite.sh` | nothing | `results/demo-date-rewrite.txt`: one change landed three ways (merge commit, squash, rebase) and the lead time each one leaves in Git |
    29	| `scripts/check-method.py` | the extracts and the clones | `results/method-checks.txt`: stage 1 cross-checked against plain git on seeded random samples, for every repo in `repos.json` |
    30	| `scripts/benchmark.sh` | the whole pipeline | `results/benchmark.txt`: machine, wall time and clone sizes of one full run |
    31	
    32	Each stage stamps its output with a hash of its own code and inputs. `02-metrics.py` refuses to mix extracts from different extract code.
    33	
    34	## The definitions
    35	
    36	- **Deploy:** every tag, release candidates included; every final release tag; days with a final release; or every first-parent commit on the default branch ("merge to main is a deploy").
    37	- **Lead time:** from the commit author date, the committer date, the time the change landed on the default branch, or the first commit of the change; to the first tag or to the first final release that contains it. Per commit or per change. A change is one landing on the default branch: a merge commit and its side branch count once, each commit of a rebased branch counts on its own, and a commit with no landing on the default branch (a backport) counts on its own from its committer date.
    38	- **Failure:** the next patch on the same `major.minor` line came within 7 days, inside the window; the release ships any commit whose subject starts with "Revert"; or a later release in the window ships a revert whose message names ("reverts commit <hash>" or "reverts CL <number>") a change this release shipped.
    39	- **Recovery:** from the failed release to the release that fixed it, for the two failure rules that name one.
    40	
    41	The performance levels come from the 2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report (v. 2024.3, page 13). They describe survey clusters, not official thresholds, and the report gives single values for change failure rate (5%, 20%, 10%, 40%), so `03-tiers.py` only places deployment frequency and lead time.
    42	
    43	## Results
    44	
    45	See `results/summary.md` for every number, and the post for what they mean. In short: each repo reaches two or three different levels for the same metric depending on the definition, and the merge button decides whether Git still knows when the work started.
    46	
    47	## Checks done on the method
    48	
    49	`scripts/check-method.py` (seeded) compares stage 1 with plain git: the side commits each merge brought in against `git rev-list --no-merges <first-parent>..<merge>` on 58 random merges, and the first final release that ships a commit against `git tag --contains` on 60 random commits. All match; see `results/method-checks.txt`.
    50

/bin/bash -lc 'nl -ba scripts/01-extract.py' in /home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Stage 1: read commit metadata and tags from each clone into results/extract/<repo>.json.
     3	
     4	Only plain git is used, so it works for any host. Nothing here decides what a
     5	deploy or a failure is; stage 2 does that from this file.
     6	"""
     7	import gzip
     8	import hashlib
     9	import json
    10	import re
    11	import subprocess
    12	import sys
    13	from datetime import datetime, timezone
    14	from pathlib import Path
    15	
    16	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    17	CONFIG = json.loads((ROOT / "repos.json").read_text())
    18	OUT = ROOT / "results" / "extract"
    19	SEP = "\x1f"
    20	YEAR = 365 * 86400
    21	
    22	
    23	def git(repo, *args):
    24	    return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), *args], check=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    25	
    26	
    27	def iso_to_ts(value):
    28	    return int(datetime.fromisoformat(value).timestamp())
    29	
    30	
    31	def code_hash():
    32	    h = hashlib.sha256()
    33	    for f in (Path(__file__), ROOT / "repos.json", ROOT / "results" / "clones.json"):
    34	        h.update(f.read_bytes())
    35	    return h.hexdigest()[:16]
    36	
    37	
    38	def read_tags(repo, pattern):
    39	    fmt = SEP.join(["%(refname:short)", "%(objecttype)", "%(objectname)", "%(*objectname)", "%(creatordate:iso-strict)"])
    40	    rx = re.compile(pattern)
    41	    tags = []
    42	    for line in git(repo, "for-each-ref", "refs/tags", f"--format={fmt}").splitlines():
    43	        name, otype, obj, peeled, created = line.split(SEP)
    44	        m = rx.match(name)
    45	        if not m:
    46	            continue
    47	        nums = [int(g) if g else 0 for g in m.groups()[:3]]
    48	        tags.append({
    49	            "name": name,
    50	            "annotated": otype == "tag",
    51	            "commit": peeled if otype == "tag" else obj,
    52	            # Annotated: the tagger date. Lightweight: the tagged commit's committer date.
    53	            "ts": iso_to_ts(created),
    54	            "major": nums[0], "minor": nums[1], "patch": nums[2],
    55	            "prerelease": bool(m.groups()[3]),
    56	        })
    57	    tags.sort(key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["name"]))
    58	    return tags
    59	
    60	
    61	def rev_list(repo, *args):
    62	    """Commits as dicts: sha, parents, author ts, committer ts, subject."""
    63	    fmt = SEP.join(["%H", "%P", "%at", "%ct", "%s"])
    64	    rows = []
    65	    for line in git(repo, "log", f"--format={fmt}", *args).splitlines():
    66	        sha, parents, at, ct, subject = line.split(SEP, 4)
    67	        rows.append({"sha": sha, "parents": parents.split(), "at": int(at), "ct": int(ct), "subject": subject})
    68	    return rows
    69	
    70	
    71	def shipped(repo, tag, earlier):
    72	    """Commits reachable from tag and from none of the earlier tags."""
    73	    if earlier:
    74	        return rev_list(repo, tag["commit"], "--not", *[t["commit"] for t in earlier])
    75	    return rev_list(repo, tag["commit"])
    76	
    77	
    78	def landings_with_side_commits(repo, branch, since, until):
    79	    """First-parent commits on the default branch, oldest first, each with the
    80	    commits it brought in. A merge brings the commits of its second parent that
    81	    no earlier landing brought; other commits bring only themselves."""
    82	    graph = {c["sha"]: c for c in rev_list(repo, branch, f"--since={since - YEAR}")}
    83	    firsts = rev_list(repo, "--first-parent", "--reverse", branch, f"--since={since}", f"--until={until}")
    84	    mainline = {m["sha"] for m in firsts}
    85	    # Everything already on the branch before the first landing counts as
    86	    # landed, so the first merge in the slice does not absorb older history.
    87	    assigned = set()
    88	    if firsts:
    89	        stack = list(firsts[0]["parents"][:1])
    90	        while stack:
    91	            sha = stack.pop()
    92	            if sha in assigned or sha not in graph:
    93	                continue
    94	            assigned.add(sha)
    95	            stack.extend(graph[sha]["parents"])
    96	    out = []
    97	    for m in firsts:
    98	        brought = []
    99	        if len(m["parents"]) > 1:
   100	            stack = list(m["parents"][1:])
   101	            while stack:
   102	                sha = stack.pop()
   103	                if sha in assigned or sha in mainline or sha not in graph:
   104	                    continue
   105	                assigned.add(sha)
   106	                c = graph[sha]
   107	                if len(c["parents"]) == 1:
   108	                    brought.append({"sha": sha, "at": c["at"], "ct": c["ct"]})
   109	                stack.extend(c["parents"])
   110	        out.append({**m, "brought": brought})
   111	    return out
   112	
   113	
   114	def read_reverts(repo, since):
   115	    fmt = SEP.join(["%H", "%at", "%ct", "%s", "%b"]) + "\x1e"
   116	    reverts = []
   117	    for rec in git(repo, "log", "--all", "-i", "--grep=^Revert", f"--since={since}", f"--format={fmt}").split("\x1e"):
   118	        rec = rec.strip("\n")
   119	        if not rec:
   120	            continue
   121	        sha, at, ct, subject, body = rec.split(SEP, 4)
   122	        target_sha = re.search(r"reverts? commit ([0-9a-f]{7,40})", body, re.I)
   123	        target_cl = re.search(r"reverts? CL (\d+)", body, re.I)
   124	        reverts.append({
   125	            "sha": sha, "at": int(at), "ct": int(ct), "subject": subject,
   126	            "target_sha": target_sha.group(1) if target_sha else None,
   127	            "target_cl": target_cl.group(1) if target_cl else None,
   128	        })
   129	    return reverts
   130	
   131	
   132	def gerrit_change_map(repo, since, wanted):
   133	    """Gerrit only: change number -> commit, from the Reviewed-on trailer."""
   134	    out = git(repo, "log", "--all", f"--since={since}",
   135	              "--format=%H %(trailers:key=Reviewed-on,valueonly,separator=%x20)")
   136	    found = {}
   137	    for line in out.splitlines():
   138	        parts = line.split()
   139	        if len(parts) >= 2:
   140	            m = re.search(r"/\+/(\d+)", parts[1])
   141	            if m and m.group(1) in wanted:
   142	                found.setdefault(m.group(1), parts[0])
   143	    return found
   144	
   145	
   146	def main():
   147	    start = iso_to_ts(CONFIG["window"]["start"])
   148	    end = iso_to_ts(CONFIG["window"]["end"])
   149	    OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   150	    clones = json.loads((ROOT / "results" / "clones.json").read_text())
   151	    for r in CONFIG["repos"]:
   152	        repo = ROOT / "data" / "repos" / r["name"]
   153	        branch = f"origin/{r['branch']}"
   154	        tags = read_tags(repo, r["tag_pattern"])
   155	
   156	        # A final release also gets the set measured against earlier final
   157	        # releases only, because a release candidate is not a production deploy.
   158	        deploys = []
   159	        for i, t in enumerate(tags):
   160	            if not (start <= t["ts"] < end):
   161	                continue
   162	            entry = {**t, "commits": shipped(repo, t, tags[:i])}
   163	            if not t["prerelease"]:
   164	                entry["commits_vs_finals"] = shipped(repo, t, [p for p in tags[:i] if not p["prerelease"]])
   165	            deploys.append(entry)
   166	
   167	        # Landings from a year before the window: stage 2 needs them to know when
   168	        # a change shipped early in the window first landed.
   169	        landings = landings_with_side_commits(repo, branch, start - YEAR, end)
   170	        reverts = read_reverts(repo, start - YEAR)
   171	        wanted = {rv["target_cl"] for rv in reverts if rv["target_cl"]}
   172	        cl_map = gerrit_change_map(repo, start - 2 * YEAR, wanted) if wanted else {}
   173	
   174	        extract = {
   175	            "repo": r["name"],
   176	            "merge_style": r["merge_style"],
   177	            "head": clones[r["name"]]["head"],
   178	            "window": CONFIG["window"],
   179	            "code_hash": code_hash(),
   180	            "generated_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
   181	            "all_tags": tags,
   182	            "deploys": deploys,
   183	            "landings": landings,
   184	            "reverts": reverts,
   185	            "cl_map": cl_map,
   186	        }
   187	        path = OUT / f"{r['name']}.json.gz"
   188	        with gzip.open(path, "wt") as f:
   189	            json.dump(extract, f)
   190	        in_window = sum(start <= m["ct"] < end for m in landings)
   191	        print(f"{r['name']}: {len(deploys)} tags in window ({sum(not d['prerelease'] for d in deploys)} final), "
   192	              f"{in_window} landings on {r['branch']}, {len(reverts)} reverts since a year before -> "
   193	              f"{path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
   194	    return 0
   195

/bin/bash -lc 'nl -ba scripts/01-extract.py' in /home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Stage 1: read commit metadata and tags from each clone into results/extract/<repo>.json.
     3	
     4	Only plain git is used, so it works for any host. Nothing here decides what a
     5	deploy or a failure is; stage 2 does that from this file.
     6	"""
     7	import gzip
     8	import hashlib
     9	import json
    10	import re
    11	import subprocess
    12	import sys
    13	from datetime import datetime, timezone
    14	from pathlib import Path
    15	
    16	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    17	CONFIG = json.loads((ROOT / "repos.json").read_text())
    18	OUT = ROOT / "results" / "extract"
    19	SEP = "\x1f"
    20	YEAR = 365 * 86400
    21	
    22	
    23	def git(repo, *args):
    24	    return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(repo), *args], check=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    25	
    26	
    27	def iso_to_ts(value):
    28	    return int(datetime.fromisoformat(value).timestamp())
    29	
    30	
    31	def code_hash():
    32	    h = hashlib.sha256()
    33	    for f in (Path(__file__), ROOT / "repos.json", ROOT / "results" / "clones.json"):
    34	        h.update(f.read_bytes())
    35	    return h.hexdigest()[:16]
    36	
    37	
    38	def read_tags(repo, pattern):
    39	    fmt = SEP.join(["%(refname:short)", "%(objecttype)", "%(objectname)", "%(*objectname)", "%(creatordate:iso-strict)"])
    40	    rx = re.compile(pattern)
    41	    tags = []
    42	    for line in git(repo, "for-each-ref", "refs/tags", f"--format={fmt}").splitlines():
    43	        name, otype, obj, peeled, created = line.split(SEP)
    44	        m = rx.match(name)
    45	        if not m:
    46	            continue
    47	        nums = [int(g) if g else 0 for g in m.groups()[:3]]
    48	        tags.append({
    49	            "name": name,
    50	            "annotated": otype == "tag",
    51	            "commit": peeled if otype == "tag" else obj,
    52	            # Annotated: the tagger date. Lightweight: the tagged commit's committer date.
    53	            "ts": iso_to_ts(created),
    54	            "major": nums[0], "minor": nums[1], "patch": nums[2],
    55	            "prerelease": bool(m.groups()[3]),
    56	        })
    57	    tags.sort(key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["name"]))
    58	    return tags
    59	
    60	
    61	def rev_list(repo, *args):
    62	    """Commits as dicts: sha, parents, author ts, committer ts, subject."""
    63	    fmt = SEP.join(["%H", "%P", "%at", "%ct", "%s"])
    64	    rows = []
    65	    for line in git(repo, "log", f"--format={fmt}", *args).splitlines():
    66	        sha, parents, at, ct, subject = line.split(SEP, 4)
    67	        rows.append({"sha": sha, "parents": parents.split(), "at": int(at), "ct": int(ct), "subject": subject})
    68	    return rows
    69	
    70	
    71	def shipped(repo, tag, earlier):
    72	    """Commits reachable from tag and from none of the earlier tags."""
    73	    if earlier:
    74	        return rev_list(repo, tag["commit"], "--not", *[t["commit"] for t in earlier])
    75	    return rev_list(repo, tag["commit"])
    76	
    77	
    78	def landings_with_side_commits(repo, branch, since, until):
    79	    """First-parent commits on the default branch, oldest first, each with the
    80	    commits it brought in. A merge brings the commits of its second parent that
    81	    no earlier landing brought; other commits bring only themselves."""
    82	    graph = {c["sha"]: c for c in rev_list(repo, branch, f"--since={since - YEAR}")}
    83	    firsts = rev_list(repo, "--first-parent", "--reverse", branch, f"--since={since}", f"--until={until}")
    84	    mainline = {m["sha"] for m in firsts}
    85	    # Everything already on the branch before the first landing counts as
    86	    # landed, so the first merge in the slice does not absorb older history.
    87	    assigned = set()
    88	    if firsts:
    89	        stack = list(firsts[0]["parents"][:1])
    90	        while stack:
    91	            sha = stack.pop()
    92	            if sha in assigned or sha not in graph:
    93	                continue
    94	            assigned.add(sha)
    95	            stack.extend(graph[sha]["parents"])
    96	    out = []
    97	    for m in firsts:
    98	        brought = []
    99	        if len(m["parents"]) > 1:
   100	            stack = list(m["parents"][1:])
   101	            while stack:
   102	                sha = stack.pop()
   103	                if sha in assigned or sha in mainline or sha not in graph:
   104	                    continue
   105	                assigned.add(sha)
   106	                c = graph[sha]
   107	                if len(c["parents"]) == 1:
   108	                    brought.append({"sha": sha, "at": c["at"], "ct": c["ct"]})
   109	                stack.extend(c["parents"])
   110	        out.append({**m, "brought": brought})
   111	    return out
   112	
   113	
   114	def read_reverts(repo, since):
   115	    fmt = SEP.join(["%H", "%at", "%ct", "%s", "%b"]) + "\x1e"
   116	    reverts = []
   117	    for rec in git(repo, "log", "--all", "-i", "--grep=^Revert", f"--since={since}", f"--format={fmt}").split("\x1e"):
   118	        rec = rec.strip("\n")
   119	        if not rec:
   120	            continue
   121	        sha, at, ct, subject, body = rec.split(SEP, 4)
   122	        target_sha = re.search(r"reverts? commit ([0-9a-f]{7,40})", body, re.I)
   123	        target_cl = re.search(r"reverts? CL (\d+)", body, re.I)
   124	        reverts.append({
   125	            "sha": sha, "at": int(at), "ct": int(ct), "subject": subject,
   126	            "target_sha": target_sha.group(1) if target_sha else None,
   127	            "target_cl": target_cl.group(1) if target_cl else None,
   128	        })
   129	    return reverts
   130	
   131	
   132	def gerrit_change_map(repo, since, wanted):
   133	    """Gerrit only: change number -> commit, from the Reviewed-on trailer."""
   134	    out = git(repo, "log", "--all", f"--since={since}",
   135	              "--format=%H %(trailers:key=Reviewed-on,valueonly,separator=%x20)")
   136	    found = {}
   137	    for line in out.splitlines():
   138	        parts = line.split()
   139	        if len(parts) >= 2:
   140	            m = re.search(r"/\+/(\d+)", parts[1])
   141	            if m and m.group(1) in wanted:
   142	                found.setdefault(m.group(1), parts[0])
   143	    return found
   144	
   145	
   146	def main():
   147	    start = iso_to_ts(CONFIG["window"]["start"])
   148	    end = iso_to_ts(CONFIG["window"]["end"])
   149	    OUT.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   150	    clones = json.loads((ROOT / "results" / "clones.json").read_text())
   151	    for r in CONFIG["repos"]:
   152	        repo = ROOT / "data" / "repos" / r["name"]
   153	        branch = f"origin/{r['branch']}"
   154	        tags = read_tags(repo, r["tag_pattern"])
   155	
   156	        # A final release also gets the set measured against earlier final
   157	        # releases only, because a release candidate is not a production deploy.
   158	        deploys = []
   159	        for i, t in enumerate(tags):
   160	            if not (start <= t["ts"] < end):
   161	                continue
   162	            entry = {**t, "commits": shipped(repo, t, tags[:i])}
   163	            if not t["prerelease"]:
   164	                entry["commits_vs_finals"] = shipped(repo, t, [p for p in tags[:i] if not p["prerelease"]])
   165	            deploys.append(entry)
   166	
   167	        # Landings from a year before the window: stage 2 needs them to know when
   168	        # a change shipped early in the window first landed.
   169	        landings = landings_with_side_commits(repo, branch, start - YEAR, end)
   170	        reverts = read_reverts(repo, start - YEAR)
   171	        wanted = {rv["target_cl"] for rv in reverts if rv["target_cl"]}
   172	        cl_map = gerrit_change_map(repo, start - 2 * YEAR, wanted) if wanted else {}
   173	
   174	        extract = {
   175	            "repo": r["name"],
   176	            "merge_style": r["merge_style"],
   177	            "head": clones[r["name"]]["head"],
   178	            "window": CONFIG["window"],
   179	            "code_hash": code_hash(),
   180	            "generated_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
   181	            "all_tags": tags,
   182	            "deploys": deploys,
   183	            "landings": landings,
   184	            "reverts": reverts,
   185	            "cl_map": cl_map,
   186	        }
   187	        path = OUT / f"{r['name']}.json.gz"
   188	        with gzip.open(path, "wt") as f:
   189	            json.dump(extract, f)
   190	        in_window = sum(start <= m["ct"] < end for m in landings)
   191	        print(f"{r['name']}: {len(deploys)} tags in window ({sum(not d['prerelease'] for d in deploys)} final), "
   192	              f"{in_window} landings on {r['branch']}, {len(reverts)} reverts since a year before -> "
   193	              f"{path.relative_to(ROOT)}")
   194	    return 0
   195	
   196	
   197	if __name__ == "__main__":
   198	    sys.exit(main())

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""Cross-check stage 1 against plain git on random samples (seeded), for every repo in repos.json.
     3	
     4	1. The side commits a merge brought in must equal
     5	   `git rev-list --no-merges <first parent>..<merge>`.
     6	2. The first final release that ships a commit must equal the earliest final
     7	   tag that `git tag --contains` lists for it.
     8	Output: results/method-checks.txt. Exits 1 on any mismatch.
     9	"""
    10	import gzip
    11	import json
    12	import random
    13	import re
    14	import subprocess
    15	import sys
    16	from datetime import datetime
    17	from pathlib import Path
    18	
    19	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
    20	SEED = 7
    21	
    22	
    23	def git(repo, *args):
    24	    return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", str(ROOT / "data" / "repos" / repo), *args],
    25	                          check=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    26	
    27	
    28	def load(repo):
    29	    with gzip.open(ROOT / "results" / "extract" / f"{repo}.json.gz", "rt") as f:
    30	        return json.load(f)
    31	
    32	
    33	def main():
    34	    rng = random.Random(SEED)
    35	    config = json.loads((ROOT / "repos.json").read_text())
    36	    start = int(datetime.fromisoformat(config["window"]["start"]).timestamp())
    37	    lines, failures = [], 0
    38	    for repo in [r["name"] for r in config["repos"]]:
    39	        ex = load(repo)
    40	        merges = [m for m in ex["landings"] if len(m["parents"]) > 1 and m["ct"] >= start]
    41	        sample = rng.sample(merges, min(25, len(merges)))
    42	        ok = 0
    43	        for m in sample:
    44	            want = set(git(repo, "rev-list", "--no-merges", f"{m['parents'][0]}..{m['sha']}").split())
    45	            ok += want == {c["sha"] for c in m["brought"]}
    46	        failures += len(sample) - ok
    47	        if sample:
    48	            lines.append(f"{repo}: side commits match git rev-list for {ok} of {len(sample)} random merges")
    49	
    50	        finals = {t["name"]: t for t in ex["all_tags"] if not t["prerelease"]}
    51	        pairs = [(c["sha"], d["name"]) for d in ex["deploys"] if not d["prerelease"]
    52	                 for c in d["commits_vs_finals"] if len(c["parents"]) == 1]
    53	        sample = rng.sample(pairs, min(15, len(pairs)))
    54	        ok = 0
    55	        for sha, name in sample:
    56	            tags = [finals[t] for t in git(repo, "tag", "--contains", sha).split() if t in finals]
    57	            first = min(tags, key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["name"]))["name"] if tags else None
    58	            ok += first == name
    59	        failures += len(sample) - ok
    60	        lines.append(f"{repo}: first final release matches git tag --contains for {ok} of {len(sample)} random commits")
    61	    text = "\n".join(lines) + "\n"
    62	    (ROOT / "results" / "method-checks.txt").write_text(text)
    63	    print(text, end="")
    64	    return 1 if failures else 0
    65	
    66	
    67	if __name__ == "__main__":
    68	    sys.exit(main())
     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# Record what a full run costs on this machine: hardware, wall time, clone size.
     3	set -euo pipefail
     4	cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
     5	{
     6	  echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
     7	  echo "machine: $(tr -d '\0' < /proc/device-tree/model 2>/dev/null || uname -m)"
     8	  echo "kernel: $(uname -sr)"
     9	  echo "git: $(git --version)"
    10	  echo "python: $(python3 --version)"
    11	  s=$(date +%s.%N)
    12	  ./run.sh > /dev/null
    13	  e=$(date +%s.%N)
    14	  printf 'run.sh wall time: %.1f s\n' "$(echo "$e - $s" | bc -l)"
    15	  echo "clone sizes:"
    16	  du -sh data/repos/* | sed 's/^/  /'
    17	  echo "  total: $(du -sh data/repos | cut -f1)"
    18	} | tee results/benchmark.txt

 succeeded in 0ms:
    20	HOTFIX_DAYS = 7  # a release followed this soon by the next patch on the same line counts as failed
    21	
    22	
    23	def iso_to_ts(value):
    24	    return int(datetime.fromisoformat(value).timestamp())
    25	
    26	
    27	def pct(values, q):
    28	    """Nearest-rank percentile; values need not be sorted."""
    29	    if not values:
    30	        return None
    31	    s = sorted(values)
    32	    k = max(0, min(len(s) - 1, math.ceil(q / 100 * len(s)) - 1))
    33	    return s[k]
    34	
    35	
    36	def summary(values_seconds):
    37	    if not values_seconds:
    38	        return {"n": 0, "median_h": None, "p75_h": None}
    39	    return {
    40	        "n": len(values_seconds),
    41	        "median_h": round(statistics.median(values_seconds) / HOUR, 2),
    42	        "p75_h": round(pct(values_seconds, 75) / HOUR, 2),
    43	    }
    44	
    45	
    46	def metrics_for(ex):
    47	    start = iso_to_ts(ex["window"]["start"])
    48	    end = iso_to_ts(ex["window"]["end"])
    49	    weeks = (end - start) / (7 * DAY)
    50	
    51	    deploys = ex["deploys"]
    52	    finals = [d for d in deploys if not d["prerelease"]]
    53	    landings_all = ex["landings"]
    54	    landings = [m for m in landings_all if start <= m["ct"] < end]
    55	
    56	    # When each commit first landed on the default branch, and the landing it came with.
    57	    landed = {}
    58	    for m in landings_all:
    59	        landed.setdefault(m["sha"], m)
    60	        for c in m["brought"]:
    61	            landed.setdefault(c["sha"], m)
    62	
    63	    # ---- Deployment frequency -------------------------------------------------
    64	    release_days = {datetime.fromtimestamp(d["ts"], timezone.utc).date() for d in finals}
    65	    df = {
    66	        "every_tag": len(deploys),
    67	        "final_tags": len(finals),
    68	        "release_days": len(release_days),
    69	        "default_branch_landings": len(landings),
    70	    }
    71	    df = {k: {"count": v, "per_week": round(v / weeks, 4)} for k, v in df.items()}
    72	
    73	    # ---- Change lead time (deploy = final release tag) ------------------------
    74	    per_commit_author, per_commit_committer, per_commit_author_no_backports = [], [], []
    75	    per_landing_from_landing, per_landing_from_first_commit = {}, {}
    76	    backports = 0
    77	    for d in finals:
    78	        for c in d["commits_vs_finals"]:
    79	            if len(c["parents"]) > 1:
    80	                continue  # merge commits carry no change of their own
    81	            per_commit_author.append(d["ts"] - c["at"])
    82	            per_commit_committer.append(d["ts"] - c["ct"])
    83	            m = landed.get(c["sha"])
    84	            if m is not None:
    85	                per_commit_author_no_backports.append(d["ts"] - c["at"])
    86	            if m is None:
    87	                # Never landed on the default branch: a backport or a release-branch commit.
    88	                backports += 1
    89	                key, land_ts, first_at = c["sha"], c["ct"], c["at"]
    90	            else:
    91	                key = m["sha"]
    92	                land_ts = m["ct"]
    93	                first_at = min([x["at"] for x in m["brought"]] or [m["at"]])
    94	            # One value per landing (pull request or change), at its first release.
    95	            if key not in per_landing_from_landing:
    96	                per_landing_from_landing[key] = d["ts"] - land_ts
    97	                per_landing_from_first_commit[key] = d["ts"] - first_at
    98	
    99	    # Lead time if every tag counted, release candidates included: each commit at
   100	    # the first tag that contained it.
   101	    first_tag_author = [d["ts"] - c["at"] for d in deploys for c in d["commits"] if len(c["parents"]) == 1]
   102	
   103	    # Lead time if every landing on the default branch counted as a deploy.
   104	    landing_as_deploy, landing_merge, landing_single = [], [], []
   105	    for m in landings:
   106	        first_at = min([x["at"] for x in m["brought"]] or [m["at"]])
   107	        landing_as_deploy.append(m["ct"] - first_at)
   108	        (landing_merge if len(m["parents"]) > 1 else landing_single).append(m["ct"] - first_at)
   109	
   110	    lt = {
   111	        "commit_author_date_first_tag": summary(first_tag_author),
   112	        "commit_author_date": summary(per_commit_author),
   113	        "commit_committer_date": summary(per_commit_committer),
   114	        "commit_author_date_without_backports": summary(per_commit_author_no_backports),
   115	        "change_from_landing": summary(list(per_landing_from_landing.values())),
   116	        "change_from_first_commit": summary(list(per_landing_from_first_commit.values())),
   117	        "landing_is_deploy": summary(landing_as_deploy),
   118	    }
   119	    # The same measure split by how the change landed: a merge commit keeps the
   120	    # side branch and its dates, a squash or rebase writes one new commit.
   121	    landing_split = {
   122	        "merge_commit_landings": summary(landing_merge),
   123	        "single_commit_landings": summary(landing_single),
   124	        # Squash and direct pushes: the author date is the landing time, to the minute.
   125	        "single_commit_landings_author_within_60s_of_committer": sum(
   126	            1 for m in landings if len(m["parents"]) == 1 and abs(m["ct"] - m["at"]) < 60),
   127	    }
   128	
   129	    # ---- Change failure rate and recovery (deploy = final release tag) --------
   130	    # 1. Hotfix: the next release on the same major.minor line came within HOTFIX_DAYS.
   131	    by_line = {}
   132	    for t in ex["all_tags"]:
   133	        if not t["prerelease"]:
   134	            by_line.setdefault((t["major"], t["minor"]), []).append(t)
   135	    hotfix_failed, hotfix_recovery = [], []
   136	    for d in finals:
   137	        line = sorted(by_line[(d["major"], d["minor"])], key=lambda t: (t["ts"], t["patch"]))
   138	        # Only fixes released inside the window, like the revert rule.
   139	        later = [t for t in line if t["patch"] > d["patch"] and d["ts"] <= t["ts"] < end]
   140	        if later and later[0]["ts"] - d["ts"] <= HOTFIX_DAYS * DAY:
   141	            hotfix_failed.append(d["name"])
   142	            hotfix_recovery.append(later[0]["ts"] - d["ts"])
   143	
   144	    # 2. The release ships at least one revert commit.
   145	    has_revert = [d["name"] for d in finals
   146	                  if any(c["subject"].lower().startswith("revert") for c in d["commits_vs_finals"])]
   147	
   148	    # 3. A later release reverts a change this release shipped.
   149	    shipped_in = {}
   150	    for d in sorted(finals, key=lambda x: x["ts"]):
   151	        for c in d["commits_vs_finals"]:
   152	            shipped_in.setdefault(c["sha"], d)
   153	    revert_shas = {rv["sha"]: rv for rv in ex["reverts"]}
   154	
   155	    def resolve(rv):
   156	        if rv["target_sha"]:
   157	            for sha in shipped_in:
   158	                if sha.startswith(rv["target_sha"]):
   159	                    return sha
   160	            return None
   161	        if rv["target_cl"]:
   162	            return ex["cl_map"].get(rv["target_cl"])
   163	        return None
   164	
   165	    reverted_failed, reverted_recovery = {}, []
   166	    unresolved_by_parser, target_not_in_earlier_release = 0, 0
   167	    for d in finals:
   168	        for c in d["commits_vs_finals"]:
   169	            rv = revert_shas.get(c["sha"])
   170	            if not rv or len(c["parents"]) > 1 or not c["subject"].lower().startswith("revert"):
   171	                continue  # same candidates as rule 2: a non-merge commit whose subject starts with Revert
   172	            if not rv["target_sha"] and not rv["target_cl"]:
   173	                unresolved_by_parser += 1
   174	                continue
   175	            target = resolve(rv)
   176	            if target is None or target not in shipped_in or shipped_in[target]["ts"] >= d["ts"]:
   177	                # Reverted before it reached a release in the window, or shipped earlier.
   178	                target_not_in_earlier_release += 1
   179	                continue
   180	            origin = shipped_in.get(target)
   181	            if origin is None or origin["ts"] >= d["ts"] or origin["name"] in reverted_failed:
   182	                continue
   183	            reverted_failed[origin["name"]] = d["name"]
   184	            reverted_recovery.append(d["ts"] - origin["ts"])
   185	
   186	    n = len(finals)
   187	    cfr = {
   188	        "hotfix_within_7_days": {"failed": len(hotfix_failed), "of": n,
   189	                                 "rate": round(len(hotfix_failed) / n, 3) if n else None},
   190	        "ships_a_revert": {"failed": len(has_revert), "of": n,
   191	                           "rate": round(len(has_revert) / n, 3) if n else None},
   192	        "shipped_change_later_reverted": {"failed": len(reverted_failed), "of": n,
   193	                                          "rate": round(len(reverted_failed) / n, 3) if n else None,
   194	                                          "reverts_unresolved_by_parser": unresolved_by_parser,
   195	                                          "reverts_of_unreleased_or_older_changes": target_not_in_earlier_release},
   196	    }
   197	    recovery = {
   198	        "hotfix_within_7_days": summary(hotfix_recovery),
   199	        "shipped_change_later_reverted": summary(reverted_recovery),
   200	    }
   201	
   202	    return {
   203	        "repo": ex["repo"],
   204	        "merge_style": ex["merge_style"],
   205	        "head": ex["head"],
   206	        "code_hash": ex["code_hash"],
   207	        "weeks": round(weeks, 2),
   208	        "deployment_frequency": df,
   209	        "lead_time": lt,
   210	        "landing_is_deploy_by_style": landing_split,
   211	        "backport_commits": backports,
   212	        "change_failure_rate": cfr,
   213	        "recovery_time": recovery,
   214	        "details": {
   215	            "hotfix_failed": hotfix_failed,
   216	            "ships_a_revert": has_revert,
   217	            "reverted": reverted_failed,
   218	        },
   219	    }
   220	
   221	
   222	def main():
   223	    results = []
   224	    hashes = set()
   225	    for path in sorted(EXTRACT.glob("*.json.gz")):
   226	        with gzip.open(path, "rt") as f:
   227	            ex = json.load(f)
   228	        hashes.add(ex["code_hash"])
   229	        results.append(metrics_for(ex))
   230	    if len(hashes) != 1:
   231	        print(f"refusing: extracts come from different code versions {hashes}", file=sys.stderr)
   232	        return 1
   233	    out = {"generated_at": datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
   234	           "extract_code_hash": hashes.pop(),
   235	           "metrics_code_hash": hashlib.sha256(Path(__file__).read_bytes()).hexdigest()[:16],
   236	           "hotfix_days": HOTFIX_DAYS, "repos": results}
   237	    (ROOT / "results" / "metrics.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2) + "\n")
   238	    for r in results:
   239	        print(f"== {r['repo']} ({r['merge_style']})")
   240	        print("  deploys/week:", {k: v["per_week"] for k, v in r["deployment_frequency"].items()})
   241	        print("  lead time median h:", {k: v["median_h"] for k, v in r["lead_time"].items()})
   242	        print("  CFR:", {k: v["rate"] for k, v in r["change_failure_rate"].items()})
   243	        print("  recovery median h:", {k: v["median_h"] for k, v in r["recovery_time"].items()})
   244	    return 0
   245	
    35	    "shipped_change_later_reverted": "a later release reverts a change it shipped",
    36	}
    37	
    38	
    39	def df_level(per_week):
    40	    if per_week > 7:
    41	        return "Elite"
    42	    if per_week >= 1:
    43	        return "High"
    44	    if per_week >= 1 / WEEKS_PER_MONTH:
    45	        return "Medium"
    46	    if per_week >= 1 / WEEKS_PER_SIX_MONTHS:
    47	        return "Low"
    48	    return "below Low"
    49	
    50	
    51	def lt_level(hours):
    52	    if hours is None:
    53	        return "n/a"
    54	    if hours < 24:
    55	        return "Elite"
    56	    if hours < 24 * 7:
    57	        return "High"
    58	    if hours < 24 * 30:
    59	        return "Medium"
    60	    if hours < 24 * 182.5:
    61	        return "Low"
    62	    return "below Low"
    63	
    64	
    65	def fmt_hours(h):
    66	    if h is None:
    67	        return "n/a"
    68	    if h < 48:
    69	        return f"{h:.1f} h"
    70	    return f"{h / 24:.1f} d"
    71	
    72	
    73	def main():
    74	    metrics = json.loads((ROOT / "results" / "metrics.json").read_text())
    75	    out = {"source": "2024 Accelerate State of DevOps report, v. 2024.3, p. 13",
    76	           "metrics_code_hash": metrics["metrics_code_hash"], "repos": []}
    77	    md = ["# Results", "", f"Window: {metrics['repos'][0]['weeks']} weeks. "
    78	          f"Levels: 2024 DORA report, p. 13. Code: extract {metrics['extract_code_hash']}, "
    79	          f"metrics {metrics['metrics_code_hash']}.", ""]
    80	    for r in metrics["repos"]:
    81	        rec = {"repo": r["repo"], "merge_style": r["merge_style"], "df": {}, "lt": {}}
    82	        md += [f"## {r['repo']} ({r['merge_style']}, head {r['head'][:12]})", "",
    83	               "| Deploy means | Deploys | Per week | Level |", "| --- | ---: | ---: | --- |"]
    84	        for k, v in r["deployment_frequency"].items():
    85	            lvl = df_level(v["per_week"])
    86	            rec["df"][k] = {**v, "level": lvl}
    87	            md.append(f"| {DF_LABELS[k]} | {v['count']} | {v['per_week']:.2f} | {lvl} |")
    88	        md += ["", "| Lead time measured from | Changes | Median | p75 | Level (median) |",
    89	               "| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |"]
    90	        for k, v in r["lead_time"].items():
    91	            lvl = lt_level(v["median_h"])
    92	            rec["lt"][k] = {**v, "level": lvl}
    93	            md.append(f"| {LT_LABELS[k]} | {v['n']} | {fmt_hours(v['median_h'])} | {fmt_hours(v['p75_h'])} | {lvl} |")
    94	        md += ["", "| A release failed if | Failed | Of | Rate |", "| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |"]
    95	        for k, v in r["change_failure_rate"].items():
    96	            md.append(f"| {CFR_LABELS[k]} | {v['failed']} | {v['of']} | {v['rate'] * 100:.1f}% |")
    97	        rec["cfr"] = r["change_failure_rate"]
    98	        rec["recovery"] = r["recovery_time"]
    99	        md += ["", "Recovery time (median): " + ", ".join(
   100	            f"{CFR_LABELS[k]}: {fmt_hours(v['median_h'])} (n={v['n']})" for k, v in r["recovery_time"].items()), ""]
   101	        df_levels = sorted({v["level"] for v in rec["df"].values()})
   102	        lt_levels = sorted({v["level"] for v in rec["lt"].values()})
   103	        rec["df_levels_reached"] = df_levels
   104	        rec["lt_levels_reached"] = lt_levels
   105	        md += [f"Deployment frequency levels reached: {', '.join(df_levels)}. "
   106	               f"Lead time levels reached: {', '.join(lt_levels)}.", ""]
   107	        out["repos"].append(rec)
   108	    (ROOT / "results" / "tiers.json").write_text(json.dumps(out, indent=2) + "\n")
   109	    (ROOT / "results" / "summary.md").write_text("\n".join(md) + "\n")
   110	    print("\n".join(md))

**Seven fixed; five partly fixed.** The displayed metric values, revised **86.1%**, **58/58** and **60/60** checks, demo output, and DORA table match the supplied evidence. No scripts, tests, or SQL were run.

`post` below means [the reviewed article](/tmp/claude-1000/-home-biliev-projects-devops-daily/f84689a1-064c-4f2a-9937-e3b6665620ab/scratchpad/wt-dora/content/posts/dora-metrics-from-git-history.md).

| Earlier finding | Status | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Different cohorts presented as identical commits | **Fixed** | `post:111` explicitly distinguishes the populations; chart now says “same history.” |
| 2. Inconsistent failure follow-up windows | **Fixed** | `02-metrics.py:139` restricts hotfixes to the window; uv is correctly **87/101 = 86.1%**. |
| 3. Landing/per-change definitions | **Partly** | Grouping and bounded lookup are explained, but `post:117` and `03-tiers.py:27` still label the mixed clock “landing on [default branch].” Its 605 unmatched commits use committer dates. |
| 4. Unresolved reverts described as naming no target | **Partly** | Counters and **21/19** values are corrected. But `post:197` still concludes “Git alone cannot tell” from parser failure; a differently formatted target can remain readable in Git. |
| 5. Timestamp guarantees | **Partly** | `post:172` adds the necessary caveat. However, `post:32` still calls committer time “landing time” and labels the whole single-parent Prometheus group “squashed.” Neither follows from the recorded statistics. |
| 6. Custom-repository workflow | **Partly** | Checker now follows configuration, and the example tag matches. But `post:231` calls the fourth capture group optional while `01-extract.py:55` unconditionally indexes it: a three-group pattern crashes. |
| 7. Unsupported factual claims | **Partly** | Benchmark now records **137.3 seconds**, Pi **4**, and **132M**. “Common” definitions/rules and named-vendor capabilities remain unsupported by these files (`post:81,184,205–210`). |
| 8. Unproven direction of censoring bias | **Fixed** | The downward-bias assertion is removed. |
| 9. Lookback-boundary attribution | **Fixed** | `01-extract.py:85–95` seeds previously reachable history. Verified by inspection; saved random checks still exclude that boundary. |
| 10. Go frequency rounding | **Fixed** | Code, results, and post now give **0.58/week**. |
| 11. Over-specific repository/release labels | **Fixed** | The cited labels now say landings, almost-linear history, first tag including beta/rc, and nearly every release. |
| 12. Incorrect nearest-rank percentile | **Fixed** | `02-metrics.py:32` now uses `ceil(...)-1`. |

**Additional concrete problems:**

- **Medium — Reconfiguring an existing checkout can still fail.** [02-metrics.py:225](/home/biliev/projects/dora-from-git/scripts/02-metrics.py:225) loads every saved extract, including repositories removed from configuration. Their old configuration-dependent hashes conflict with newly generated extracts, causing rejection. Filter by configured repositories or remove obsolete extracts.
- **Low — Intro overstates tier coverage.** `post:24` says “placed every result” into performance levels; only deployment frequency and lead time are classified, as `post:73` correctly acknowledges.
